     - *Measure*: ripple P as in the ripple check, on crops with median voiced f0 ≥ 250 Hz (where the ripple sits) and on all crops.
     - *Reading*, per set, on jit2 / jit0 with a 95 % paired bootstrap interval over crops: upper bound < 0.5 → "jitter removes it (hop–pulse locking)"; lower bound > 0.75 → "not jitter"; otherwise "partly".
     - *Also reported*: Spearman correlation between each crop's ripple (its own P) and its clip's drawn jitter on base, with a bootstrap interval.
     - *Expectation*: P jit0 > regen > jit2.
  2. *The step between the 60 and 62 ms offsets (Parakeet's input length)*. Hypothesis: Parakeet's layer 5 changes where its input length crosses an encoder-frame (80 ms) boundary as the offset grows. A sharp step needs most crops to share one input length: true of synthetic crops (used alone) and of VocalSet (long files, so nearly every crop gets the full 0.96 s after it), not of Zeroth-Korean (short utterances, varying context).
     - *Arms*: synthetic crops drawn with 80 ms of extra clip audio (`n_frames` = 408; zeros where the clip ends). The student input is as base. Parakeet's input ends at the crop's end (**end0**), 40 ms later (**end40**), or a random 0–78 ms later, in 2 ms steps seeded per crop (**endR**).
     - *Measure*: R² by offset with each line's 10 ms pattern removed; the step at offset o is the mean over the four offsets from o minus the mean over the four before; o* is the most negative.
     - *Reading*, per set: end40's o* within ±4 ms of (end0's o* + 40) mod 80 → "tied to the input length"; within ±4 ms of end0's o* → "not the input length"; otherwise "unclear". endR's largest step is reported against end0's (it should shrink if the step is tied to length).
  3. *Context length (Parakeet only, real audio)*. Long streams: Zeroth-Korean val, VocalSet val and LibriTTS-R dev-clean, each speaker's or singer's files joined in order; 64 segments of 4 s per corpus from streams of at least 30 s, seeded.
     - Parakeet's layer 5 on the segment's encoder frames inside windows of 4, 8, 16 and 30 s centred on it. Window starts stay on the segment's 80 ms grid, so the frames are the same; the window is clamped inside the stream.
     - *Measure*: R² of each window's frames against the 30 s window's (per-dimension variance over the 30 s frames), for the middle 2 s of the segment and for its first and last 0.5 s.
     - Reported only, no reading. It says how far Parakeet's layer 5 on 4 s crops (the students' and H5's training targets) is from Parakeet on long audio.
  - *Amendment before any follow-up result (Hannah, 2026-10-26: aperiodic voices).* Two more arms for follow-up 1, built like the jitter arms:
    - **hnr0**: aspiration noise fixed at 0 dB HNR. Fixing `hnr_db` at the drawn value reproduces a clip exactly (checked), so this changes only the noise level. *Reading*: hnr0 / regen, thresholds as for jit2 / jit0 ("aspiration removes it" / "not aspiration" / "partly").
    - **whisper**: the whole clip whispered (`p_whisper_clip` = 1, no glottal periods). This changes the rest of the generator's draws (different formants), so it is not paired. Reported as P on all crops against regen, with unpaired bootstrap intervals, and no reading.
    - The synthesiser has no creak, diplophonia or subharmonic source.
    - Also added to the tests: clips regenerate exactly from the flat store, and fixing jitter keeps the random stream.
  - *Follow-up 1 result (2026-10-26, `ripple_v2_12k_v4_10k_follow1.txt`; v4)*. regen reproduces base exactly (max |R² difference| 0.00000).
    - *Readings*: jit2 / jit0 "partly" on three of four (f0 ≥ 250 Hz and all crops, both sets), "not jitter" on synthetic v2 with all crops (2.04 [0.79, 4.02]); hnr0 / regen "partly" on all four.
    - More jitter gave *more* ripple, not less (point ratios 1.5–2.5). Whisper (no glottal periods) has the same P as regen (0.0035 against 0.0037; unpaired 0.94 [0.28, 4.17]). The ripple doesn't track a crop's drawn jitter (Spearman −0.03 to +0.02, intervals within ±0.2). Hop–pulse locking is not supported.
    - *Seen after the readings (post hoc), and decisive for the whole ripple question: a noise floor.* P (a peak-to-peak of five means) is positive even with no effect. Its null, with the five sub-offsets shuffled within each crop's hops (200–300 shuffles):
      - The original observation (cached crops, 128): v4 synthetic v2 P 0.0038 against a null mean of 0.0033 (95th percentile 0.0052); v2s 0.0083 against 0.0060 (0.0094); VocalSet 0.0012 against 0.0023; Zeroth-Korean 0.0003 against 0.0004.
      - The f0 500–700 Hz subset (45 crops): 0.0105–0.0115 against 0.0114–0.0121; its "tripling" is the smaller sample.
      - The arms: within the null's 95 % everywhere except jit2 on synthetic v2 (0.0051 against 0.0039) and hnr0 on v2s (0.0054 against 0.0047). Split-half agreement of the five-point pattern is weak (r −0.35 to +0.74).
      - So the 10 ms ripple across crops is not distinguishable from sampling noise. The ripple check and these arms were testing noise. The noise floor should have been estimated before either was registered.
    - *What is real*: how much one crop's R² moves between neighbouring 2 ms offsets. The SD within a hop, per crop, is 0.011 (synthetic v2), 0.014 (v2s) and 0.013 (VocalSet) against 0.0024 (Zeroth-Korean), and 0.018–0.020 at f0 500–700 Hz. Individual crops are sensitive to small shifts, more so at high f0 and with singing, but not in a pattern shared across crops. This is what made the synthetic lines look rough.
  - *Follow-up 2 result (2026-10-26, `ripple_v2_12k_v4_10k_follow2.txt`; v4): **"unclear"** on both sets under the rule.* end0 reproduces the step on a fresh crop draw at the same offset (o* 62 ms; −7.4 and −6.3 × 10⁻³). end40's largest step is at 70 ms (synthetic v2) and 28 ms (v2s), against 22 ms predicted. endR's largest step is about half of end0's (−3.6 and −3.2 × 10⁻³).
    - *Seen after the readings (post hoc; the noise check the new AGENTS.md rule asks for, done late)*: the step at 62 ms, with 95 % bootstrap intervals over crops:
      - end0: −7.4 [−11.6, −3.1] and −6.3 [−11.3, −0.4] × 10⁻³;
      - end40: +1.0 [−2.3, +4.2] and +2.0 [−2.1, +6.4];
      - endR: +0.0 [−4.0, +4.0] and −0.1 [−4.0, +4.2];
      - paired, end40 − end0: +8.4 [+5.7, +11.4] and +8.2 [+5.1, +11.6]; endR − end0: +7.5 [+5.2, +10.1] and +6.1 [+2.8, +9.3].
    - So the step is real, replicates across crop draws, and vanishes when Parakeet's input ends anywhere else: it depends on where Parakeet's input ends. It does not simply move 40 ms with a 40 ms longer input, so the registered "encoder-frame boundary" mechanism is not confirmed. The exact dependence on length is open.
    - *Relevance*: v4's synthetic training targets are Parakeet on the crop alone, shifted by 10φ ms, so every crop shares one input length per phase. This step sits between phases 6 and 7. It is about 0.007 in R², small next to the students' gaps to Parakeet.
  - *Follow-up 3 result (2026-10-26, `context_length_parakeet.txt` / `.csv`; reported only; segment margin 14 s rather than the registered 13 s, so the 30 s window always fits)*. Parakeet's layer 5 on a 4 s segment, R² against the 30 s window (middle 2 s / first 0.5 s / last 0.5 s):

    | Corpus | 4 s | 8 s | 16 s |
    |---|---|---|---|
    | LibriTTS-R dev-clean | 0.85 / 0.80 / 0.74 | 0.95 / 0.95 / 0.95 | 0.98 / 0.98 / 0.98 |
    | Zeroth-Korean val | 0.72 / 0.65 / 0.70 | 0.90 / 0.91 / 0.92 | 0.96 / 0.97 / 0.97 |
    | VocalSet val | 0.14 / 0.09 / 0.07 | 0.41 / 0.41 / 0.41 | 0.71 / 0.71 / 0.70 |

    Intervals are within ±0.04 for speech and ±0.2 for VocalSet.
    - Parakeet on a 4 s crop is far from Parakeet on long audio: R² 0.72–0.85 for speech and 0.14 for singing. Even 16 s is not converged for singing. Near the window's edges it is lower still (speech 0.65–0.80).
    - The students' targets and H5's training features are Parakeet on 4 s crops, and the gold sets are scored on whole short files. All of these are a different function from Parakeet on long recordings.
    - *Seen after the result (post hoc)*: Parakeet's feature extractor normalises every mel band by its mean and variance over the whole input (`ParakeetFeatureExtractor`, "normalize mel features, ignoring padding"). Part or most of the window dependence may therefore come from the normalisation statistics, not from attention. Singing streams (long silences, crescendi, messa di voce, changing tasks) would move those statistics most. Not yet separated.
- *Pre-registered 2026-10-26 (Hannah), after follow-up 3's result, before running: normalisation or attention? (reported only).* Question: how much of the window dependence in follow-up 3 comes from `ParakeetFeatureExtractor` normalising each mel band over the whole input, and how much from attention?
  - *Checked before registering (AGENTS.md)*: the effect is large (R² 0.14–0.85 against 1), far above noise. The normaliser reaches every frame. Inside a window, the short window's features equal the 30 s window's up to a per-band scale and shift (white-noise check at the 4 s segment: median |difference| 0.003 after z-scoring at the right offset, 0.87 one frame off). Only the first and last two or three frames differ, because their STFT uses padding.
  - *Arm*, the same segments as follow-up 3 (same seed): for each window w of 4, 8 and 16 s, the window's own features converted to the 30 s window's statistics, then the encoder on the w-second window alone (attention unchanged). The conversion fits the per-band scale and shift by least squares on the interior frames (all but three at each end) against the 30 s features at the same frames, and applies it to every frame of the window. The edge frames stay as the short window computed them, so only the statistics change. **own**: as follow-up 3, recomputed in the same run.
  - *Measure*: R² against the 30 s window, as in follow-up 3 (middle 2 s, first and last 0.5 s).
  - *Reading*, per corpus at 4 s on the middle 2 s: the gap to 1 under stats30 against own. stats30's gap ≤ 0.25 × own's → "normalisation accounts for most"; ≥ 0.75 × own's → "attention accounts for most"; otherwise "both". Reported with 95 % bootstrap intervals over segments. 8 and 16 s reported the same way.
  - *Bug in the first run, fixed before any reading (2026-10-26)*. The stats30 arm fed the encoder an all-ones attention mask. `ParakeetFeatureExtractor` marks its last frame as padding (800 of 801 on 8 s), and attending to that one frame moves layer 5 by up to 77, so the first run's stats30 numbers (R² far below own) are void. Fixed to use the extractor's mask. Checked before rerunning: the direct path reproduces the normal one exactly, and the conversion reproduces the 30 s features on interior frames (max |difference| 4e-7). *Seen in passing*: the size of that one-frame effect is itself evidence of how sensitive Parakeet's layer 5 is to its input's edge.
  - *Result (2026-10-26, `context_length_stats30.txt` / `.csv`; rerun with the mask fixed, numbers within 0.03 of the void run): **"attention accounts for most"** under the rule, on every corpus.* stats30's gap to 1 is 2.5–9.7× own's (95 % intervals 2.1–12.6; all above 0.75). R² falls, for example at 4 s on the middle 2 s: LibriTTS-R 0.85 → 0.23, Zeroth-Korean 0.72 → −0.40, VocalSet 0.14 → −1.36.
    - *The arm is confounded, which should have been seen before registering.* Parakeet was trained only on inputs normalised over themselves (zero mean, unit variance per band, in every input). Under the 30 s statistics a short window no longer is, so its input is off-distribution. A large loss is then expected whatever the window dependence comes from, and the reading does not show that attention is the cause.
    - *What it does show*: normalising with statistics from a longer span makes Parakeet's output on a short window much worse, not closer. Fixed or long-window normalisation statistics are not a way to make short-window targets match long-audio Parakeet. Parakeet has to be run on the span it is meant to describe.
    - *For any later target or feature cache (to be decided and pre-registered)*: choose one context convention for Parakeet (for example, whole files, or windows of at least 16 s with the frames used taken from the middle). Use it for student targets, head features and scoring alike. Follow-up 3 suggests 4 s crops are far from it for singing.
- *Pre-registered 2026-10-26 (Hannah), before running: does H5's F1 correction depend on Parakeet's input span? (reported only).* Context: follow-up 3 showed Parakeet's layer 5 depends strongly on its input span. H5's heads were trained on Parakeet over 4 s crops, the women's bias is measured on whole PTDB-TUG utterances (a few seconds), and v4 also learned Parakeet over short whole-word files (0.5–1.9 s). Hypothesis: H5's correction holds in sentence-length or longer spans and collapses in short ones. A student pulled toward short-span Parakeet (v4) would then lose it, which could explain part of v2 against v4 (PTDB-TUG fidelity v2 0.49, v4 0.42; Hillenbrand 0.10 against 0.47).
  - *Checked before registering (AGENTS.md)*: across H5's six seeds the women's cut has an SD of about 1 Hz, and the comparison is paired by seed (the same heads in every regime), against a cut of 5.3 Hz. The regime reaches the measure: the bias is computed from Parakeet's layer 5 on exactly the span varied.
  - *Regimes* for the gold_ptdb_cp set (479 dev utterances, closed-phase reference frames). Parakeet's layer 5 is recomputed on the same 80 ms frames, and everything else in the scoring cache is copied from `emb_v1` (labels, teacher v3's columns, and the other sets' layer 5):
    - **whole**: each utterance alone, as `emb_v1`. A check that recomputation reproduces it.
    - **short**: each utterance cut into consecutive 1.04 s windows (13 encoder frames) on its 80 ms grid, each run alone, with each window's frames put back in place. This is near the word items' span.
    - **long**: each utterance inside its speaker's other dev utterances (in order, cyclically), at least 8 s before and 8 s after, with the preceding audio trimmed so the utterance starts on the 80 ms grid. Its frames are taken from the middle.
  - *Measure*: `eval_heads.py` with D-033's six H5 heads, ensemble (c) with teacher v3: women's closed-phase |F1 bias| cut, men's rise, and Hillenbrand (unchanged across regimes). Six-seed means, and per-seed differences against whole.
  - *Reading* on the women's cut, short − whole (six paired seeds, 95 % t-interval):
    - mean ≤ −3 Hz with the interval below 0 → "H5's correction depends on the span (lost in short spans)";
    - |mean| < 1.5 Hz with the interval inside ±3 → "span-independent";
    - otherwise "unclear".
  - long − whole is reported the same way, without a reading.
  - *Result (2026-10-26, `d034_spans_{whole,short,long}.txt`, `d034_spans_build.txt`): **"span-independent"**.*
    - whole reproduces `emb_v1` (layer 5 R² 1.0000, max |difference| 0.06) and its numbers (+5.3 ± 1.1).
    - Six-seed women's cut: short +5.1 ± 1.1, long +5.4 ± 0.7.
    - Paired by seed: short − whole −0.2 Hz [−1.7, +1.2] (95 % t-interval); long − whole +0.1 Hz [−0.8, +1.0].
    - Men's rise −1.8 (short) and −2.1 (long), against −2.8.
    - This holds although layer 5 itself changes a great deal: R² against whole is −0.59 for short and 0.67 for long.
    - The heads alone barely move either: women's closed-phase F1 median error, mean over seeds, whole −5.3, short −3.8, long −4.4 Hz. MAE 66–68 (whole, long) against 71–77 (short).
    - *So*: H5 reads F1 from directions of layer 5 that hardly depend on the input span, while most of layer 5's variance does. The span hypothesis for v2 against v4 is not supported: short-span Parakeet keeps H5's correction, so learning it would not by itself have cost v4 the cut.
    - *Seen in passing (post hoc)*: whole-vector R² against Parakeet, the fidelity measure used throughout D-034, is dominated by span-dependent variance that the F1 head ignores. Students can gain or lose fidelity without it bearing on head transfer (v4 against v2 on Hillenbrand: 0.47 against 0.10, with no gain in the cut). A fidelity measured in the subspace the heads read would be the better proxy.
- *Pre-registered 2026-10-26 (Hannah), before running: head-subspace fidelity (reported only, no decision).* Context: the span test showed H5 reads F1 from a span-robust part of layer 5, while whole-vector R² is dominated by variance the heads ignore. Question: do the students reproduce Parakeet in the subspace the heads read, and is each student's error kept out of that subspace, spread like noise, or concentrated in it?
  - *Features* (80 ms frames, the four sets `eval_heads.py` reads):
    - **P**: Parakeet layer 5 (`emb_v1`);
    - **v2**: student v2 at 12,000, 80 ms means; **v2n**: its matched-noise control (P plus Gaussian noise with v2's per-dimension error variance; `student_as_parakeet.py`, rebuilt from `cache/stc_v2`, and checked against the existing `stc_v2_80ms_scoring`);
    - **v4**: the 20k branch, 80 ms means; **v4n**: its noise control (`stc_v4_80ms_scoring`).
  - *Heads*, six seeds each: H5 (D-033, trained on P), S80 (v2's 80 ms heads, stage 1c), S4x80 (v4's 80 ms heads, the verdict's). The 10 ms heads are deferred: they would need Parakeet at all eight phases on the gold audio.
  - *Measures*, at the reference frames: PTDB-TUG closed phase (women and men separately), Hillenbrand, and synthetic v2 and v2s.
    - **Subspace fidelity**: R² of the head's input projection (LayerNorm, then the 1024→128 linear map, interpolated to the frame as the head does) on the features against the same on P. Per-dimension R² with P's variance, averaged over the 128 dimensions.
    - **Whole-vector R²** at the same frames, for comparison.
    - **F1 read-out agreement**: median |F1 on the features − F1 on P| in cents, head alone.
    - **Bias**: `eval_heads.py`'s full report for every features × heads cell (15 cells; ensemble (c) with teacher v3, as always).
  - *Checked before registering (AGENTS.md)*: six seeds per head family, compared paired by seed between each student and its noise control. The projection is exactly what each head applies to its input, so features reach the head only through it (and the LayerNorm's per-frame scaling).
  - *Readings*:
    1. Per student (v2, v4) and head family, on PTDB-TUG women's closed-phase frames: Δ = subspace R²(student) − subspace R²(its noise), six-seed mean with a 95 % t-interval. Above 0 → "error kept out of the head's subspace"; below 0 → "error concentrated in it"; otherwise "like noise".
    2. Student heads on P: S80 and S4x80 on Parakeet under the part-2 rule on six-seed means. "Pass" → "the heads learned a mapping that works on Parakeet (the student's features are what's off)"; "fail" → "the heads learned something else".
    - Everything else is reported without a reading.
  - *Result (2026-10-26, `d034_subspace.txt` / `.csv`, `d034_subspace_bias_{P,v2,v2n,v4,v4n}.txt`).* The rebuilt v2 caches match the existing ones exactly.
    - **Reading 1: "error kept out of the head's subspace"** for both students and every head family. On PTDB-TUG women, subspace R² minus the noise control's: v2 +0.050 / +0.034 / +0.026 (H5 / S80 / S4x80), v4 +0.070 / +0.048 / +0.053, all intervals within ±0.004.
    - **Reading 2: "the heads learned something else"** for both student-head families on P (part-2 rule on means: no). S80 on P: women's cut +2.1, men's rise −3.9. S4x80 on P: +5.2 and +6.4 (fails on men).
    - *Bias in every cell* (women's cut / men's rise, six-seed means). H5: P +5.3 / −2.8, v2 +5.6 / −5.0, v2n +4.9 / −1.4, v4 +0.4 / −4.5, v4n +4.8 / −1.3. S4x80: P +5.2 / +6.4, v2 +5.8 / +3.8, v4 +1.3 / −0.6, v4n +5.7 / +7.7.
    - *Signed errors (post hoc)*: v4's features shift every head's F1 read-out down against v4n by about 10–16 Hz, for both sexes (head alone, PTDB-TUG closed phase). H5: women −19.5 against −7.4, men −8.7 against +4.9. v2 shows no such shift for women. With H5, the ensemble on v4 lands at −7.2 Hz for women, so the women's cut is about 0 by overshoot, not by a missing correction. S4x80 reads men about 20 Hz high on P: v4's own heads partly learned to undo v4's offset.
    - *F1 read-out against P* (median |difference|, H5 alone): PTDB-TUG women v2 37, v4 55, v4n 58 cents; Hillenbrand v2 67, v4 45, v4n 41.
    - *So*: v4's error in the heads' subspace is smaller than noise of the same energy, but it is directional. It lies along the read-out direction, shifting F1, where noise only scatters it. R² (even in the head's subspace) does not see direction. The offset's history from the H5 sweep (head alone, student minus noise, women / men): v2 4k −13 / −10, 8k +2 / −9, 12k +2 / −7; v4 4k −31 / −22, 8k −8 / −10, 12k −13 / −12, 16k −17 / −18, 10k branch −14 / −15, 20k branch −12 / −14. Both students start low; v2 recovers for women; v4 recovers partly by 8k, then drifts back down, whether its rate stays constant or decays (the 10k branch decays from 8k).
- *Pre-registered 2026-10-26 (Hannah), before running: two causes of v4's F1 offset that need no training (reported only).*
  - (a) *Phase averaging*: the diagnostic feeds v4's mean over eight per-frame outputs. Each was trained to match Parakeet at one 10 ms phase, so the mean approximates Parakeet averaged over phases, not Parakeet's own 80 ms frame, which H5 was trained on.
    - *Arm*: PTDB-TUG gold_ptdb_cp with layer 5 replaced by Parakeet averaged as the diagnostic pools the student. Parakeet runs on each utterance shifted by 10q ms (q = 0..7). The average at encoder frame e is over q = 0..3 at frame e and q = 4..7 at frame e − 1 (mels 8e − 4 .. 8e + 3). Other sets as `emb_v1`; q = 0 alone must reproduce `emb_v1`.
    - *Reading*: H5 alone, F1 median signed error, phase-averaged minus P, averaged over women and men, per seed (six seeds, 95 % t-interval). ≤ −6 Hz with the interval below 0 → "phase averaging explains at least half of v4's offset"; within ±3 Hz with the interval inside ±6 → "not phase averaging"; otherwise "partly".
  - (b) *Level dependence*: Parakeet normalises each input, so it is level-invariant; the student's VQT log-power is not.
    - *Arms*: v4 (20k branch) and v2 (12k) on PTDB-TUG audio at −10 dB and +10 dB (`student_cache.py` with a new `VA_GAIN_DB`, gold files only), pooled and noise-free as the diagnostic, scored with H5. 0 dB is the existing cell.
    - *Reading*, per student: H5 alone, F1 median signed error at +10 dB minus at −10 dB, averaged over women and men, per seed. |mean| ≥ 10 Hz with the interval excluding 0 → "level-dependent"; |mean| < 4 Hz with the interval inside ±8 → "level-invariant"; otherwise "partly".
  - *Checked before registering (AGENTS.md)*: both comparisons are paired by head seed, so head-to-head spread cancels. The noise level of a paired difference is a few Hz, against the 12 Hz offset. Both causes reach the measure: (a) is exactly the diagnostic's pooling; (b) shifts the student's log-power input by a constant.
  - *Schedule (Hannah's question; recorded, not run)*: a cosine-annealed v4 branch from 6k–8k to 12k would test the schedule. The 10k branch, which decays linearly from 8k, already moved the offset further down (women −8 → −14), as much as 4,000 more constant-rate steps did. So the schedule is unlikely to be the cause. It stays a candidate if (a) and (b) both come back empty.
  - *Result (2026-10-26, `d034_offset_a.txt`, `d034_offset_b_{v2,v4}_{-10,10}.txt`).* q = 0 reproduces `emb_v1` to a max |difference| of 0.063 on layer 5; the phase-averaged layer 5 has R² 0.916 against Parakeet's own.
    - **(a): "partly".** Phase-averaged minus P, H5 alone, F1: −5.1 Hz [−6.2, −4.0] (women −4.5, men −5.7; seeds −3.2 to −6.4). Against v4's whole offset on the same scale (v4 minus P, −12.1 [−14.7, −9.5]), phase averaging accounts for about 40 %. It reaches only v4: v2 was trained on 80 ms means, so it never had to approximate the per-phase average.
    - **(b): "level-invariant" for both.** +10 dB minus −10 dB: v2 +0.1 [−0.9, +1.1]; v4 +3.3 [+2.6, +3.9] (consistent across seeds but under the 4 Hz bar; each 10 dB step moves v4 about 1.7 Hz).
    - *So*: about 5 of v4's 12 Hz comes from what its targets are (a mean over phases, where H5 was trained on Parakeet's own 80 ms frame); level isn't it. The other ~7 Hz is unexplained, leaving (c) word weight and the schedule. The fix for the explained part needs no training: either train v4's diagnostic heads on phase-averaged Parakeet, or pool v4 as the phase average H5 expects. Neither is registered.
- *Pre-registered 2026-10-26 (Hannah), before running: phase-aligned pooling for v4 (reported; decides v4's pooling for later ensemble work).*
  - *Arm*: v4 (20k branch, `stc_v4`) read by H5 through `student_as_parakeet.py` with `VA_POOL=aligned`. Encoder frame e takes v4's single stored frame 8e − 4 (mel 8e, phase 0: v4's targets put Parakeet frame e of the φ-shifted audio at mel 8e + φ, so φ = 0 is Parakeet's own frame). Clamped to the item at both ends. Against the existing mean of stored 8e − 8 … 8e − 1 (`stc_v4_80ms_scoring`). Its matched-noise control is rebuilt from the aligned frames' own error.
  - *Reading*: H5 alone, F1 median signed error on PTDB-TUG dev closed phase, aligned minus mean, averaged over women and men, paired per seed (six seeds, 95 % t-interval). ≥ +3 Hz with the interval above 0 → "alignment recovers the phase-averaging part" ((a) predicts about +5); within ±2 Hz with the interval inside ±4 → "no effect"; otherwise "partly". Reported beside it: fidelity R² to layer 5 (aligned against mean), ensemble (c)'s signed bias and women's cut, Hillenbrand F1 MAE, and the aligned noise control.
  - *Decision*: "recovers", with Hillenbrand F1 MAE (ensemble c) no more than 2 Hz worse than mean pooling's → aligned pooling is v4's convention for the ensemble work; otherwise mean pooling stays.
  - *Checked before registering (AGENTS.md)*: the code path (above) puts Parakeet's own frame at stored 8e − 4, inside the current window, so the change reaches exactly what (a) measured. In (a) and (b) the paired per-seed differences had an SD of 0.6–1.2 Hz (intervals ±0.6 to ±1.1), so the ±2–3 Hz bars sit above the noise level. The cost is losing the eight-frame average, which may lower fidelity; that is reported, not decided on.
  - *Result (2026-10-26, `d034_aligned_v4_{student,noise}.txt`, `_build.txt`).*
    - **Reading: "alignment recovers the phase-averaging part".** Aligned minus mean pooling, H5 alone: +5.4 Hz [+4.5, +6.4] (women +5.3, men +5.6; every seed +4.2 to +6.6), as (a)'s −5.1 predicted. The rebuilt noise control doesn't move (−0.0 [−0.2, +0.2]).
    - **Decision: aligned pooling is v4's convention for the ensemble work.** Hillenbrand F1 MAE (ensemble c) is 0.2 Hz better (cut +4.3 against +4.1).
    - *Fidelity rose instead of falling*: R² to layer 5, aligned against mean, PTDB-TUG 0.430 / 0.414, Hillenbrand 0.454 / 0.431, synthetic 0.639 / 0.622 and 0.605 / 0.591. Averaging over phases was a systematic error, larger than the noise the single frame adds.
    - *What's left*: head alone, women −14.2, men −3.1, against the aligned noise control's −7.4 / +4.9: v4 still reads 7.4 Hz [−10.7, −4.1] low (from about 12). Ensemble (c): women −4.7 (from −7.2), men +1.1; women's cut +3.0 (from +0.4), men's rise −5.3. Part-2 rule on means: still no. The residual leaves word weight (c) and the schedule.
- *Ensemble of more models: feasibility check before any pre-registration (2026-10-26, Hannah's question).* Could a larger ensemble of teachers (which read F1 high) and heads on student features (which read it low) cancel bias, as the two-member ensemble (c) does?
  - *Members*: teacher v3 and its sibling seeds (`tv3_s0`, `tv3_s2`), teacher v2 (`teacher_v2_d16k`, to be scored as `tv2`), and H5's six seeds on Parakeet, v2 (80 ms means) and v4 (aligned pooling).
  - *Computed now, on dev only*: each member's signed F1 bias (already largely seen) and the frame-level correlation of F1 errors (in log frequency) between members, PTDB-TUG closed phase by sex and Hillenbrand. Noise floor: the correlation between seeds of the same model (teacher v3 siblings; H5 seeds on the same features).
  - *Not computed until a pre-registration*: any ensemble's error or bias. The candidate arms (Hannah, 2026-10-26): a group-balanced median and a weighted mean in log frequency, with one weight set for both sexes fitted leaving one speaker out, scored against ensemble (c); Hillenbrand as the out-of-set check and one logged semi-private confirmation.
  - *Go/no-go*: if the teacher-vs-head error correlation is close to the within-group (seed) correlation, extra members only add weight to a side, and the idea moves to `docs/considering.md` instead.
  - *Result (2026-10-26, `d034_ensemble_feasibility.txt`): go, but only for two groups.* Frame-level F1 error correlation, teacher vs H5 seed (mean, with range), against the seed-to-seed noise floor:
    - PTDB-TUG women 0.78 (0.70–0.83) against 0.91–0.93; men 0.45 (0.33–0.52) against 0.79–0.85; Hillenbrand 0.58 (0.37–0.70) against 0.82–0.88. Teachers and heads err differently, most of all for men.
    - H5 on Parakeet, v2 and v4 is effectively one member. The same head seed on different features correlates 0.81–0.97, at or above different seeds on the same features (0.79–0.92), so the features change the bias and little else. Hillenbrand is the exception for v2 (H5 on v2 +19.7 Hz against +3.1 on Parakeet and +1.3 on v4).
    - Teacher v2 is a third, separate member (0.56–0.84 with teacher v3; 0.41–0.76 with H5) but reads high everywhere (women +18.3, men +16.0, Hillenbrand +27.5) and has the largest MAE.
    - Teacher v3's siblings correlate 0.82–0.94 with it: a seed mean helps a little.
    - All correlations include the reference's own error, which is common to every member; that raises them all alike and doesn't change the comparison.
    - *So*: the distinct members are teacher v3 (seed family), H5 (head family, any features) and teacher v2. An ensemble's gain will come from mixing teacher v3 and H5 in the right proportion, not from adding more models of either kind. Teacher v2 mostly adds bias.
- *Pre-registered 2026-10-26 (Hannah), before any ensemble was computed: a fitted teacher/head weight for F1.* CPU only, from the feasibility dumps. Run for two feature sets, each decided separately: H5 on Parakeet (P, slower to label) and on v4 with aligned pooling (the student).
  - *Members*: T = teacher v3; H = the mean in log frequency of H5's six seeds. F1 only, as ensemble (c).
  - *Baseline (c̄)*: 0.5 T + 0.5 H. It differs from the arms in one thing each.
  - *Arm (w)*: w T + (1 − w) H in log frequency, one w for both sexes, w on a 0.01 grid in [0, 1]. Fitted leaving one PTDB-TUG dev speaker out (12 folds: 6 women, 6 men), scored on the held-out speakers' frames, pooled. Fit objective (Hannah: bias matters most, since more data is unlikely to fix it; a light squared-error term guards the error): J(w) = mean over women and men of |median signed F1 error| + 0.1 × RMSE of F1 over both (Hz). The 0.1 keeps the error term light: 1 Hz of RMSE costs as much as 0.1 Hz of bias. Hillenbrand is scored with w fitted on all 12 speakers.
  - *Arm (m)*: the frame-wise median of teacher v3, `tv3_s0`, `tv3_s2` and H5 seeds 0–2 (three of each).
  - *Reported only*: (w) with T the three-seed teacher mean; (w) with the squared-error term off; each fold's w.
  - *Reading*, per arm and feature set: women's bias cut, men's bias rise and Hillenbrand F1 MAE cut against teacher v3 alone (D-033's part-2 rule: cut ≥ 5, rise ≤ 5, MAE cut ≥ −4 → "worth confirming"), and the arm minus (c̄) on the women's and men's |bias| with 95 % intervals from a speaker bootstrap (resampling speakers within sex, 2000 draws). "Improves on (c̄)" means the mean over women and men of |bias| (the objective's bias term) is lower, with its interval excluding 0 (amendment before running, no results seen: the registration first named both sexes without saying which decides). An arm that meets the rule and improves on (c̄) gets one logged semi-private confirmation, with w fitted on all of dev; otherwise (c) stays.
  - *Checked before registering (AGENTS.md)*: teacher and head errors correlate well below seed-to-seed (above), so a weight can trade bias without just adding one side's error. The bias difference between w = 0.5 and the fitted w is unknown, but the speaker bootstrap gives each comparison its own noise level. With 12 speakers, the fitted w can move between folds; that spread is reported.
  - *Result (2026-10-26, `d034_ensemble_weight.txt`): no arm improves on (c̄) beyond the speaker noise, so (c) stays and nothing goes to semi-private.* Women / men signed bias, then the part-2 rule against teacher v3 alone (+7.6 / +6.7, Hillenbrand MAE 38.9):
    - *P*: (c̄) −1.0 / +3.4, rule passes (cut +6.6, rise −3.3, MAE cut +5.5). (w) (w = 0.57 on all dev; folds 0.31–0.76) +0.2 / +4.1, passes; mean |bias| against (c̄) −0.1 [−0.5, +1.9]. (m) +2.7 / +5.6, fails (cut +5.0, but Hillenbrand and men worse than (c̄)).
    - *v4*: (c̄) −4.8 / +0.5, fails (cut +2.9). (w) (w = 0.72; folds 0.63–0.82) −0.2 / +3.2, passes (cut +7.4, rise −3.6, MAE cut +4.1); against (c̄) −0.9 [−2.1, +4.2]. (m) −1.7 / +3.5, passes; −0.1 [−1.8, +2.9].
    - The squared-error term never moved w by more than 0.02. The three-seed teacher (reported) behaves like teacher v3 alone.
    - *So*: on v4 a fitted weight (about 0.7 on the teacher) makes up for v4's remaining downward offset and passes the rule where an equal mix doesn't. On P the equal mix with H5's seed mean already passes. Neither gain over (c̄) is resolved: with six speakers per sex, the speaker bootstrap spans several Hz on each sex's bias (e.g. v4 women −4.5 [−5.0, +6.0]). *Post hoc, worth carrying forward*: the same speaker noise applies to every women's-cut comparison in D-033 and D-034. Their rules were taken on seed means; only D-033's full arm also ran the speaker bootstrap, which found the women's cut unresolved on dev's six women (−4.7 [−8.3, +5.3]). A cut of 5 Hz is about the width of the speaker interval. *(Corrected 2026-10-26, same day: this note first said no comparison had used a speaker bootstrap.)*

## D-035 (2026-10-26, pre-registered): anatomical vocal-tract-length head, frozen-encoder probe

Roadmap Next 4; D-021 item 4; plan §8.4's divergence from principle 1. **Question**: does a learnt per-frame anatomical-length head on frozen features give a more consistent per-speaker length on real voices than the formant-derived apparent length, without reading length from f0? And which base, teacher v3's encoder or Parakeet's layer 5, carries more of it? Code and reports on branch `vtl-probe` (parakeet worktree).

- *Labels*: the synthesiser's `length_cm` per clip (`clip_tract`, `vtl_labels.py`), attached to `cache_heads.py`'s 40,000 crops by recovering their clip ids from the seeded draws (`vtl_cache_labels.py`; 1,390 checked crops all match the cached formant labels). Only clips whose length was **not** drawn from f0 (D-020). Resynthesised crops have no label.
- *Main arms: tube clips only* (8,263 crops). One thing differs between them, the features:
  - **T**: teacher v3's (`conformer_v2r2_s1_d16k`) formant-encoder output (`Teacher.enc`, 10 ms), on the same crops regenerated from the fixed seeds (checked against the cached labels).
  - **P**: Parakeet layer 5 (80 ms; labels read at mel 8e), from the existing cache.
- *Secondary arm* **T+c, reported only**: T's features, tube and cascade clips (13,895 crops). Cascade clips have no well-defined anatomical length: their F1–F3 are the men's Peterson & Barney table scaled uniformly by 17.5 / L, the uniform scaling D-022 found wrong for women and children. So they are kept separate (Hannah, 2026-10-26): never mixed into the main arms' labels, and given no anatomical-length label in item 5. The arm only shows what mixing them would do.
- *Head*: per frame, with no context of its own (both encoders already have it): LayerNorm → Linear(in, 256) → GELU → Linear(256, 256) → GELU → Linear(256, 2), the mean and log variance of log2 length. Gaussian NLL on sonorant frames. AdamW 1e-3, weight decay 0.01, 300 warm-up steps, cosine over 4,000 steps, batch 32 crops. Three seeds per arm; the arm's estimate is the seed mean (σ² = mean of σ² + variance of the means).
- *Aggregation* (back end, plan §8.4): per file, the precision-weighted mean of log2 length over frames. On Hillenbrand, the reference frames; on synthetic clips, sonorant frames; on the probe, 0.3–0.7 s.
- *Baseline* **A**: apparent length from labeller v3's F1–F4 (`backend.vtl`, uniform tube, equal weights), calibrated linearly in log2 on the tube crops' true F1–F4 (slope 0.972), per-file median.
- *Measures* (dev speakers only):
  1. *Primary, real*: reliability on Hillenbrand dev (27 men, 29 women): per adult group, the between-speaker variance of speaker means over that plus the within-speaker variance across files; the mean of men and women. Scale-free, so shrinking every estimate towards a constant doesn't win it. Paired speaker bootstrap within group, 2,000 resamples.
  2. *Guard, real*: the women / men ratio of speaker medians in [0.85, 0.92] (anatomy 0.886, Vorperian et al. 2018). This catches a head that collapses the sexes towards its training prior.
  3. *Guard, synthetic*: the f0-invariance probe's median |slope| of log2 length against log2 f0, f0 ≤ 250 Hz, ≤ 10 cents per octave (A: 1). The whole sweep is reported.
  4. *Reported*:
     - synthetic validation (train_v2's held-out val clips, which teacher v3 never trained on): per-clip median |error| in cents, and σ coverage within 1σ and 2σ, per frame and per clip;
     - on the probe: the vowel spread (A: 107 cents) and bias by length;
     - on Hillenbrand: within-speaker SD in cents, group medians (children included), and the within-group correlation of speaker length with speaker median f0 (leakage on real voices).
- *Decision rule*:
  - **The VTL head joins item 5's retrain** if T's reliability exceeds A's with the paired interval excluding 0, T passes both guards, and its seeds agree in direction. Otherwise item 5 goes without it, and A stays the length output.
  - If T fails but P passes, that is recorded as the teacher's features limiting VTL (a case for VTL on the D-034 student), not as a change to item 5.
  - P against T is reported with paired intervals.
  - T+c against T is reported with paired intervals and decides nothing (see the secondary arm).
  - If T passes, it gets one logged semi-private confirmation on the same measures.
- *MRI reference (lockbox; not part of this decision)*:
  - **Speakers**: the USC 75-speaker real-time MRI set's 16 speakers with hand-drawn midsagittal contours in 75-Speaker Annot-16 (9 men, 7 women; 10 frames each). They have the most direct anatomical lengths of any set here, so they go to the lockbox (`tiers.LOCKBOX_SPEAKERS`, Hannah 2026-10-26) and open only at the milestone acceptance that would ship a VTL output.
  - **Reference length**: per speaker, the median over its 10 frames of the glottis-to-lips midline (`mri_vtl.py`; *moved 2026-10-28 with `mri_vtl_annot.py` to the private repo `vtl-mri`, history included; last here in `c1eb10d`*). The airway is closed into a polygon: the lip aperture; palate; the velum's oral surface to the uvula tip; the pharyngeal wall below it; the arytenoid; back along the epiglottis, tongue and lower lip. The midline is the cheapest path from the lip aperture to the glottis (see the end point below) that keeps away from the walls, crossing one only where articulators touch (57 of 160 frames, median 0.8 px). *Revision before any model was run (2026-10-26)*: the first version, a nearest-wall midline, cut through the soft palate and tongue and mis-placed the lip end. Hannah flagged examples by eye; the polygon version was checked on those examples and the worst crossings.
  - **Glottis end point, provisional (Hannah, 2026-10-26; before any model run)**: each frame's length is (A + 2 B) / 3. A ends at the midpoint of the epiglottis's and "arytenoid"'s lowest points; B is the same with the back wall cut at the epiglottis bottom's height (a level glottal line).
    - *Why*: Annot-16's "arytenoid" contour is the back wall of the laryngeal inlet, and it runs on below the glottis. Its line from the epiglottis bottom tilts 31 ± 10° (men) and 40 ± 13° (women) down at the back, ending a median 10 mm below the epiglottis bottom. Hannah's glottal lines tilt 19 ± 9° (men) and 12 ± 9° (women) up at the back.
    - *Evidence*: Hannah marked the glottis (and redrew walls where Hannah disagreed) on one random frame per speaker (`annotate/`, outside the repo; `mri_vtl_annot.py`). That is 11 frames; one was dropped as too noisy to label, and two were revised after seeing the rules' overlays, so not blind. On the 10, the maximum-likelihood mix is w = 0.35 [0.02, 0.86] on A (Gaussian) or 0.33 [0.00, 0.61] (Laplace). Per sex: men 0.88 (Gaussian; 0.46 Laplace), women 0.10 / 0.00; their intervals overlap, so one weight for everyone. Against those labels, the 1:2 mix has median error +0.04 cm and median absolute error 0.17 cm (halfway 0.20, A 0.45, B 0.34). A curvature-based end point fitted worse (0.46–0.55).
    - *Ratio*: the endpoint rule is fitted to where the glottis is seen, never to a target ratio, so the MRI women / men ratio stays a measurement. All rules give 0.900–0.908 on the full set; Hannah's own 10 labels give 0.852. Hannah's glottis on the women's frames sits higher than any rule built on Annot-16's contours, so the rule may read women about 0.2 cm long.
    - *To revisit* when more frames are annotated, including blind re-labels: the weight, and whether it differs by sex.
  - **Seen while building it** (reference labels only, no model run on these speakers' audio; 1:2 rule): men 16.91 cm (14.5–17.6), women 15.15 (13.5–15.6), medians of speaker medians; women / men 0.896 (means 16.61 / 14.97, 0.901); within-speaker IQR 0.44 cm; correlation with height +0.79 (+0.62 within sex). Endpoint uncertainty about ±0.3 cm absolute, per-frame label noise about 0.4–0.5 cm; so the measures are speaker ordering and the women / men ratio, with absolute cm reported. CT for orientation: 17.6, 15.6 cm (Vorperian et al. 2018).
  - **Measures at that opening**:
    - Spearman correlation across the 16 speakers between each estimator's per-speaker length and the MRI length (all, and within sex pooled after centring), with a speaker bootstrap;
    - the women / men ratio against the MRI's;
    - the adopted head against apparent length A, paired.
  - **Audio**: the speakers' 2D real-time MRI recordings (AAC, 22.05 kHz mono in the MP4s, scanner noise cancelled, so a hard domain; downloaded 2026-10-26, MD5 checked, kept zipped), from the main set's video archive (CC BY 4.0).
  - **Not used before that opening**: no model is run on them, no model is tuned against them, and they don't feed calibration.
  - **Scaling to the other 59 speakers** (a segmentation model trained on Annot-16's labels) is a separate roadmap item. Their tiers are set before any of them is measured.
- *Checked before registering* (AGENTS.md; `reports/analysis/vtl/feasibility_*.txt` on `vtl-probe`):
  - *Synthetic*: length is decodable from L5. A crop-mean ridge gets held-out R² 0.81 (tube 0.86, cascade 0.73), against a shuffle null of −0.03. True F1–F4 through the calibrated uniform tube reach 0.96, so on synthetic data the formant baseline is strong, and the head's case rests on real voices and on vowel invariance.
  - *Real noise floors* (Hillenbrand dev):

    | | A | Per-frame L5 ridge |
    |---|---|---|
    | Reliability, men / women | 0.17 / 0.14 (intervals about ±0.08) | 0.40 / 0.22 |
    | Within-speaker SD | 156 cents | 118 cents |
    | Women / men ratio | 0.863 [0.849, 0.891] | 0.942 |

    The ridge's men's reliability beats A's by +0.23 [+0.10, +0.35]. Its ratio of 0.942 would fail the guard (shrinkage, or an ASR encoder discarding speaker differences), so the guard bites.
  - *Probe*: A's f0 slope is 1–2 cents per octave (labeller v3's formants are f0-invariant on clean probe clips), and its vowel spread of 107 cents equals the true formants' own. That spread is the uniform tube's limit, not formant error, and it is where a learnt head can gain.
  - *Code path*: the head reads only frozen features, and the label is a pure tract quantity drawn independently of the source on the clips used.

- *Result (2026-10-27, `reports/analysis/vtl/d035_probe.txt` on `vtl-probe`): **the VTL head does not join item 5's retrain.** T passes the primary measure, the ratio guard and seed agreement, and fails only the f0-invariance guard.*

  | | T | P | Tc (reported) | A |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | Hillenbrand reliability (mean) | 0.224 | 0.297 | 0.271 | 0.153 |
  | Women / men ratio | 0.860 | 0.915 | 0.871 | 0.863 |
  | Within-speaker SD | 118 c | 106 c | 113 c | 156 c |
  | Probe median \|slope\| at f0 ≤ 250 Hz | 24.3 c/oct | 142.1 | 20.1 | 1.4 |
  | Probe vowel spread | 46 c | 65 c | 48 c | 107 c |
  | Synthetic per-clip median \|error\|, tube | 18 c | 28 c | 16 c | 27 c |

  - *Paired differences*: T − A reliability +0.071 [+0.021, +0.117]. P − T +0.073 [+0.009, +0.133]. Tc − T +0.047 [+0.021, +0.071].
  - *Seeds*: per-seed reliability T 0.225 / 0.226 / 0.217 and P 0.288–0.294, all above A's 0.153.
  - *P* fails the same guard, badly: the probe reads its length short by 178–625 cents, growing with length. So "P passes where T fails" does not apply.
  - *Within-sex correlation of speaker length with speaker f0*: T −0.30, Tc −0.37, A −0.34, P −0.19. Every estimator, including the formant baseline, finds longer tracts in lower-pitched speakers of the same sex, as anatomy predicts. On real voices, then, the heads lean on f0 no more than A does.
  - *Synthetic σ*: per frame it is roughly calibrated (T within 1σ / 2σ 0.72 / 0.97). Per clip it is far too narrow (0.02 / 0.09), because the precision-weighted mean treats correlated frames as independent. A back-end aggregate needs an effective frame count.
- *Post hoc, decides nothing (`d035_probe_null.txt`, `vtl_probe_null.py`): the guard's threshold was never checked against a learnt head's noise floor.* AGENTS.md asks for the noise floor before registering. It was taken from A, whose per-clip noise is near zero, and not from a head.
  - With f0 shuffled within each (vowel, length) group, per-clip noise alone gives T a median |slope| of 15.2 c/oct (95th percentile 21.1) and Tc 14.9 (21.8). Both are above the 10 c/oct threshold, so an f0-independent head would usually have failed it.
  - T's observed 24.3 is still beyond its null (p = 0.004): a real f0 dependence of roughly 9 c/oct above noise, about 0.1 cm per octave at 17 cm. Tc's 20.1 is not distinguishable from noise (p = 0.10). P's per-clip noise is 116 cents, so its slope says little; its length bias is the problem.
  - The registered decision stands. Whether to register a noise-calibrated guard (excess over the shuffle null, or more and longer probe clips), and re-decide in a new entry, is Hannah's call.
- *Disclosure*: before the run, a code check of the scoring script used real P head seed 0 (copied three times) and fake T features, so P's scores were previewed. P decides nothing, and the rule was fixed before.

## D-036 (2026-10-27, pre-registered): the VTL head trained jointly, in a decay branch

Registered with Hannah's agreement, including the noise-calibrated f0 guard at the bound proposed before the code check's value was seen (her call: keep the original plan regardless). Code `c0a23a7`; weight default in `losses.py`; chain `chains/d036_chain.sh`; rule `decide_d036.py`. Follows D-035, whose frozen-feature head beat the formant-derived length on Hillenbrand reliability but whose f0 guard sat below a learnt head's noise floor. Designed with Hannah. **Question**: trained jointly with the teacher in a decay branch, keeping every existing head and loss, does a VTL head read length better than D-035's frozen readout, without costing the formants, f0 or bandwidths? Ablations separate the schedule, the readout and the head's starting point.

- *Common to every arm*:
  - Branch from teacher v3's constant-phase checkpoint `conformer_v2r2_s1/ckpt_016000`: the whole model, every head and the optimiser state; teacher v3's command, data, channel and seed; 4,000 steps.
  - Every existing head and loss stays on with teacher v3's weights.
  - Evaluation is windowed (the new default) against the windowed references.
- *VTL head* (when present): D-035's per-frame design (LayerNorm → 256 → 256 → 2; mean and log variance of log2 length) on the formant trunk.
  - *Labels*: train_v2 tube clips whose length was not drawn from f0, sonorant frames; everything else masked, including resynthesised and cascade clips (Hannah: no well-defined anatomical length; D-035).
  - *Loss*: Gaussian NLL, weight **0.0125**.
  - *Own parameter group*: AdamW 1e-3, weight decay 0.01, linear warm-up over 300 steps, then cosine to 0 at the branch's end (D-035's head schedule).
  - *Gradient into the trunk*: gated, 0 for the first 300 steps (the head learns on live features but cannot move them), then a linear ramp to 1 over 700 steps, full from step 1,000 of the branch.
- *Arms*:
  - **0, control**: no VTL head; the model's rate held at 1e-3 for 300 steps, then 1 − √ decay over 3,700. Against teacher v3 it isolates the schedule.
  - **1, main**: arm 0's schedule plus a VTL head, randomly initialised (seed 0).
  - **2, frozen readout**: as 1, with D-035's teacher-arm head (seed 0) attached frozen. Can the trunk reshape itself for a fixed readout?
  - **3, standard schedule**: as 1 (same head seed), but the model decays from the branch with no hold, as teacher v3 did. Against 1 it isolates the hold.
  - **4, tepid start**: as 1, with the head starting at 0.5 × D-035's head plus 0.1 × the seed-0 fresh initialisation (shrink and perturb, Ash & Adams 2020).
- *Measures*:
  - *Formants, f0, bandwidths*: D-031's set on the branch's last evaluation and on dev.
    - Synthetic validation in SE units from the six full-size runs (F1–F4 0.71 / 0.92 / 1.58 / 2.55 Hz; RPA worse only below −0.1 point).
    - Dev guard-rails: Hillenbrand F1 ≤ +4, F2 ≤ +9, F3 ≤ +20 Hz; PTDB-TUG RPA ≥ −1.0 point; women's closed-phase |error| ≤ +5 Hz.
  - *Length*: D-035's measures from the model's own VTL output (`vtl_score_joint.py`): Hillenbrand dev reliability (primary), women / men ratio, within-speaker SD, group medians, within-sex r(length, f0); the invariance probe with its shuffle null; synthetic error and σ coverage. Paired speaker bootstrap against A (apparent length from labeller v3's formants) and between arms.
- *Decision rule*: **the joint head (with this branch's schedule, gate and weight) goes into item 5's retrain** if arm 1 passes all three:
  1. *No formant cost*: against arm 0, no F1–F4 synthetic difference beyond +2 SE, RPA not below −0.1, and every dev guard-rail held.
  2. *Length beats A*: reliability above A's (paired interval excluding 0) and women / men ratio in [0.85, 0.92].
  3. *f0 guard, now noise-calibrated*: on the probe, the median |slope| at f0 ≤ 250 Hz exceeds its shuffle null's median by at most 10 cents per octave (about 0.1 cm per octave at 17 cm). The null's p-value is reported. Excess over the null, not detectability, bounds the size of an f0 dependence, so a noisier head is not rewarded.
  - Otherwise item 5 goes without a VTL head, and A stays the length output.
  - Arm 1 against D-035's frozen teacher head is reported (does joint training improve length?).
  - Arms 0, 2, 3 and 4 are reported with paired intervals and decide nothing. D-035 stays decided as registered; the new guard applies to this entry's arms only.
- *Checked before registering* (AGENTS.md):
  - *Gradient share* (`vtl_grad_check.py`, 20 training batches at the 16k checkpoint, with D-035's fitted head as a stand-in for a trained joint head): at weight 1, the VTL loss's gradient into the trunk is 8.1× the existing losses' (IQR 6.7–12.0), because a Gaussian NLL's gradient grows as 1/σ² and the head is confident. Weight 0.0125 gives about a 10 % share. The two gradients are nearly orthogonal (median cosine +0.02), so little interference is expected. As the joint head's σ shrinks its share can grow; the global gradient clip (1.0) bounds the step.
  - *Feature drift*: D-035's head reaches an NLL of −2.94 on the 16k encoder against −3.18 on the decayed one it was trained on, so the decay changes the trunk's length-relevant features only a little. That bears on arms 2 and 4.
  - *Noise floor of the f0 guard*: D-035's shuffle null (15 c/oct for a frozen teacher head) is the reason for the new form. Its per-arm null is computed with each arm's own estimates.
  - *Code check*: the scoring script ran on a stand-in (the 16k checkpoint with D-035's head attached, i.e. arm 2 before any training). It decides nothing. *Seen before registering*: its probe excess over the shuffle null was +10.7 c/oct (observed 25.5, null median 14.8, p = 0.002). The bound of 10 was proposed before this was seen and is kept; the stand-in shows it sits near where a frozen teacher head lands.
- *Cost*: about 1.5 h per arm with evaluations, so 7–8 h. Order: 0 and 1 (decisive), then 2, 3 and 4.
- *Amendment (Hannah, 2026-10-27 08:50, before any length result or decision was seen; only arms 0–3's dev guard-rail reports and the training logs' VTL NLL had been looked at)*: **head-seed replicates of arm 1**, for a seed mean (AGENTS.md: seed-to-seed variation on real voices is large).
  - *Arms*: **1s1** = arm 1 with `vtl_seed=1`; **1s2** = `vtl_seed=2`. Nothing else changes (the same branch, data stream and schedule as arm 1), so each stays paired with arm 0. Checkpoints every 250 steps (saving only; training identical).
  - *Window*: the GPU is available until 11:30, so the queue is stopped then (`chains/d036_seeds.sh`). 1s1 should finish (about 1.5 h); 1s2 is started if there is time and resumed later from its last checkpoint. An arm only counts once complete with its measures.
  - *Measures*: as arm 1 (paired dev reports against arm 0; `vtl_score_joint.py`), in `reports/analysis/d036/vtl_joint_seeds.{txt,json}`.
  - *Use*: the registered decision stays arm 1's. Reported beside it: the seed mean over the complete head seeds of reliability's paired difference against A, the women / men ratio and the probe excess. If the seed mean would fail a criterion that arm 1 passes (or the reverse), the decision is flagged as **not robust to the head seed**, and item 5 does not take the head until a further pre-registered replicate settles it.
  - *Seen so far (training logs, synthetic VTL NLL, median over the last 500 steps)*: arm 1 −2.91, arm 2 (D-035's head, frozen) −3.00, arm 3 −2.91; arm 1's was still falling slowly at the end (−2.83 → −2.91 over the last 1,000 steps).
- *Result (2026-10-27 09:23; `reports/analysis/d036/decide_d036.txt`, `vtl_joint.txt`, paired dev reports in `reports/analysis/paired/w400/`)*. **The joint VTL head joins item 5's retrain** (arm 1 passes all three). The head-seed replicates (amendment) are still running; the decision is flagged if their seed mean disagrees.
  - *Run note*: the first run stopped after arm 0's training (03:09), when writing its paired report: `reports/analysis` had been a junction into the `voice-analyser-m3` worktree, removed during a worktree clean-up. Tracked reports were restored from git and the chain restarted (03:11); arm 0's finished checkpoint was kept, so nothing ran twice. Since then `scripts/links_into.py` (AGENTS.md).
  - *1, no formant cost*: synthetic F1–F4 against arm 0 +0.3 / −0.6 / +0.8 / −0.5 SE, RPA −0.02; dev Hillenbrand F1 / F2 / F3 +0.6 [−0.0, +1.1] / −0.5 (better) / +0.5 Hz, PTDB-TUG RPA +0.2 (better), women's closed-phase F1 −0.1. All within bounds. Arm 0 against teacher v3's own decay: every measure within noise, so the held, re-decayed schedule changes nothing by itself.
  - *2, length beats A*: Hillenbrand reliability 0.226 against A's 0.153, paired +0.074 [+0.025, +0.123]; w/m 0.868 (A 0.863), in [0.85, 0.92]. Within-speaker SD 120 c against A's 156.
  - *3, f0 guard*: probe median |slope| (f0 ≤ 250 Hz) 18.3 c/oct, shuffle null median 11.0, **excess +7.3** (bound 10); P(null ≥ observed) 0.004, so the drift is still detectable, but small.
  - *Joint against D-035's frozen teacher head (reported)*: reliability unchanged (0.226 against 0.224); f0 drift 24.3 → 18.3 c/oct; vowel spread 46 → 35 c; probe bias by length (13 / 15.5 / 18 cm) −37 / −60 / −59 c → +0 / −4 / −12 c, so joint training mostly removed the probe's level bias and some of its f0 and vowel dependence, without making Hillenbrand more reliable.
  - *Other arms (decide nothing)*: all four head arms are alike. Reliability against A +0.074 to +0.083, w/m 0.868–0.876, probe excess +4.8 to +7.3. Arm 3 (no hold) is slightly more reliable than arm 1 (+0.009 [+0.003, +0.015]), so the hold does not help. Arm 2 (D-035's head, frozen) has the lowest f0 excess (+4.8) and within-speaker SD, and the largest probe bias for long tracts (18 cm: −33 c). Arm 4 (tepid) is indistinguishable from arm 1.
  - *Group medians (reported, not a measure)*: men 16.8, women 14.6, children 13.7 cm (A: 17.8 / 15.4 / 14.7). No anatomical comparison: the MRI lengths are lockbox speakers' (D-035).
  - *Head-seed replicate (amendment; 2026-10-27 11:18; `vtl_joint_seeds.txt`, `paired/w400/d036_arm0_vs_d036_arm1s1.txt`)*. 1s1 complete; 1s2 was started with 35 minutes left and stopped at 11:30 (incomplete, so it does not count; it resumes from its checkpoint). 1s1's length scoring was run by hand at 11:16, inside the window: the queue would only have scored after 1s2 (an ordering slip in `d036_seeds.sh`).
    - *1s1 alone*: reliability against A +0.069 [+0.022, +0.118]; w/m 0.863; probe 22.8 c/oct, null median 12.5, **excess +10.2** (just above the bound 10); vowel spread 34 c; bias by length −11 / −19 / −23 c. Formants against arm 0: Hillenbrand F1 +0.0, F2 +0.9 [−0.0, +2.6], **F3 +2.3 [+0.4, +4.6] (worse; within the +20 guard-rail)**, RPA +0.0.
    - *Seed mean over the complete head seeds (0 = arm 1, and 1)*: reliability against A +0.072, w/m 0.866, probe excess **+8.8**. Every criterion holds on the mean, so the decision is **not flagged**.
    - *Reading*: the head seed moves the f0 excess by about 3 c/oct (7.3 against 10.2), so the margin under the bound is about one seed's spread. It also moves the formant guard-rails: arm 1 had F2 better and F3 within noise, 1s1 has F2 within noise and F3 worse by 2.3 Hz. Both are within bounds, but single-seed formant differences of this size are seed noise, not effects of the head. The 1s2 replicate, resumed later, tightens the mean.
  - *Second replicate, 1s2 (complete 2026-10-27 22:43; resumed from its 11:30 checkpoint in `chains/d037_prep.sh`; both seeds scored together in `vtl_joint_seeds.txt`)*: reliability against A +0.062 [+0.012, +0.105]; w/m 0.863; probe 22.4 c/oct, null 12.3, **excess +10.1**; vowel spread 29 c. Formants against arm 0: F1 −0.4, F2 +0.4 [+0.1, +0.6] (worse), **F3 +4.3 [+0.5, +9.2] (worse)**, RPA +0.2 (better).
    - *Seed mean over the three head seeds (0, 1, 2)*: reliability against A +0.068, w/m 0.865, probe excess **+9.2**. Every criterion holds on the mean, so the decision stays **not flagged**.
    - *Reading*: the margin is thin. Two of the three seeds sit just above the f0 bound on their own (10.2, 10.1); arm 1 (7.3) was the favourable seed. F3 is worse in two of three seeds (+2.3, +4.3 Hz; arm 1 +0.5, within noise), well inside the +20 guard-rail but consistent enough to suggest a small F3 cost from the joint head. Item 5 should report F3 against a no-head control and the f0 excess per seed.
  - *So*: item 5 takes the VTL head with this entry's gate, weight 0.0125 and its own warm-up. Arm 3 suggests a plain decay (no hold) is at least as good; the training NLL suggests the head is slightly under-trained (about 0.8 dB SNR short of D-035's head on synthetic data). D-037's refits (roadmap item 4) test that before item 5 is set up.

## D-037 (2026-10-27, pre-registered): refitting the VTL head on frozen trunks, and a formants → VTL cascade

*Status*: registered with Hannah's agreement (2026-10-27, 16:20), before any D-037 result. Seen beforehand: D-036's results and its 1s1 replicate; D-035's frozen heads' validation SNR (the noise-floor check below). The feature caches for arm 3's trunk were being built when it was registered (they show no result). Choices agreed with Hannah: arm 3's trunk decides (the no-hold schedule item 5 would use); every under-training measure reported; a fixed 20,000-step head schedule (a realistic end-to-end length), no early stopping; vowel classification reported only; the reverse cascade (VTL → formants) split into D-038; Q1's SNR condition as a paired clip bootstrap (below).

- *Questions*:
  1. **Is D-036's joint head under-trained?** Its training NLL was still falling at the end, about 0.8 dB SNR short of D-035's frozen head. A head refitted for much longer on the same frozen trunk, against the trunk's own joint head, answers it on identical features.
  2. **Did joint training make better length features?** The same refit on teacher v3's trunk (no VTL training) and on each joint trunk.
  3. **Formants → VTL**: does the VTL head gain from the trunk's own F1–F4 as inputs?
- *Trunks* (the formant encoder's output `h`, 128-d, 10 ms, fp32 as `analyse()`): teacher v3 (`conformer_v2r2_s1_d16k`, D-035's cached features reused); `d036_arm3` (joint, fresh head, no hold; **decides**); `d036_arm1` (joint, fresh head, held schedule; reported, as a check that the answers agree); `d036_arm2` (joint, D-035's head frozen; reported).
- *Features* (`vtl_cache.py` gains a checkpoint argument): per trunk, D-035's four parts (train_T, val, probe, hb), each frame with `h`, the trunk's own F1–F4 (expected position, log2 Hz) and, for the joint trunks, the joint VTL head's mean and log σ. Teacher v3's existing parts get only the new columns, checked against the cached `h`.
  - *A larger training cache* (Hannah, 2026-10-27): D-035's T heads trained on 8,263 tube crops (its train_T's 13,895 include cascade crops), about 15 passes in 4,000 steps; 20,000 steps on that cache would be about 77, while the joint arms never see a crop twice. A refit that memorised the cache could come out worse than the joint head and read as "not under-trained". So train_T is rebuilt for every trunk, teacher v3's included, from **all 29,356 train_v2 training clips that are tube clips with length not drawn from f0, each under two independent channel draws: 58,712 crops** (each clip is shorter than a 400-frame crop, so a clip gives one crop per channel draw), about 11 passes over the crops at 20,000 steps × 32. Each frame keeps its formant and sonorant labels (for D-038). Disk: about 6 GB per trunk, 24 GB in all (58 GB free).
- *Heads*: D-035's MLP and recipe (AdamW 1e-3, wd 0.01, 300 warm-up steps, cosine, batch 32 crops, Gaussian NLL on sonorant frames, tube crops with length not drawn from f0), three seeds each, with one change: **20,000 steps** instead of 4,000. Training and validation NLL are logged every 500 steps; they stop nothing.
  - *Overfitting flag* (fixed now): a head is **flagged** if its final validation NLL is more than 0.05 nats (about 0.4 dB SNR) above its own minimum over the run, i.e. validation turned up while training kept falling. Seed means are reported with and without flagged seeds. If any R3 seed is flagged, Q1's answer is reported as **unreliable** and item 5 keeps D-036's setup until a rerun settles it.
- *Arms*:
  - **RT** (teacher v3's trunk), **R3** (arm 3's), **R1** (arm 1's), **R2** (arm 2's): `h` alone.
  - **R3c**, **R1c**: as R3, R1 with the trunk's own F1–F4 (log2 Hz, detached, standardised with training-set statistics) appended to `h`.
- *Measures*:
  - *Under-training (question 1; all reported)*: on train_v2's held-out crops, validation NLL, **SNR** (dB: 20 log10 of the labels' log2 SD over the head's σ), per-clip |error| by clip kind and σ coverage, for each refit and for its trunk's own joint head; the training and validation curves and their gap; the overfitting flags; RT against D-035's T (4,000 steps, same trunk): does longer training help a frozen-feature head at all?
  - *Length (questions 1–3)*: D-036's set from `vtl_score.py`. Probe median |slope| at f0 ≤ 250 Hz, shuffle null, excess; vowel spread; bias by length. Hillenbrand dev reliability (primary), w/m, within-speaker SD, group medians, r(length, f0) within sex. Seed means, paired speaker bootstrap.
  - *Vowel classification (reported only; first use, noise floor unknown)*: Hillenbrand dev's 12 vowels from the trunk's own F1–F3 at the reference frames (log Hz), four ways: raw; minus log2 of the speaker's length from a given estimate (median over the speaker's *other* files, so a token's own vowel cannot enter); the same with A; Lobanov (per speaker and formant z-score) as the reference. Linear discriminant analysis with speakers held out (5 folds grouped by speaker); accuracy with a speaker bootstrap. The classifier never touches a model.
- *Decision rules* (arm 3's trunk):
  - *Q1 → item 5's head schedule*: the joint head is **under-trained** if R3 beats arm 3's own head on validation SNR **by at least 0.5 dB, with the paired clip-bootstrap interval of the gain excluding 0** (amended below) (2,000 resamples of the qualifying validation clips, the same resample for both; R3 as the seed mean, μ the mean of the seeds' means and σ² the mean of their variances plus the variance of their means, as D-035; SNR from the resampled clips' mean NLL), **and** on Hillenbrand reliability (paired interval excluding 0). Then item 5 holds the VTL head's rate at its peak until the model's last 25 %, then decays it. Otherwise item 5 keeps D-036's setup.
  - *Q3 → the VTL head's inputs*: R3c joins item 5's design if, against R3: reliability improves (paired interval excluding 0); probe excess ≤ 10 c/oct and rises by at most 3 (so the cascade cannot nearly double the f0 drift and still pass); w/m in [0.85, 0.92]; vowel spread rises by at most 10 c.
  - *Q2, and every arm 1 / arm 2 comparison*: reported only. If arm 1's answer to Q1 or Q3 differs from arm 3's, that is noted beside the decision.
  - *If both Q3 and D-038's rule pass*, the two cascades would form a loop (each head reading the other). Item 5 then takes only the one with the larger gain against its own control, in units of its paired interval's half-width; a two-pass design would need its own entry.
- *To check before registering* (AGENTS.md):
  - *Noise floors*: the head-seed spread of validation SNR and reliability (D-035's T seeds 0.225 / 0.226 / 0.217; D-036's 1s1 / 1s2 for the joint head). Both of Q1's conditions are paired intervals, so they carry their own sampling noise.
    - *Done (2026-10-27, `scripts/analysis/d037_noise_floor.py`, CPU)*: D-035's three T heads on the 146 qualifying validation clips: SNR 21.19 / 21.13 / 21.06 dB, so the head-seed SD is **0.06 dB** (NLL 0.007 nats) and the bound max(1 dB, 2 SD) is simply 1 dB, about 16 SDs. The clip sample matters more: the two halves of the validation clips differ by about 0.8 dB for every seed, but R3 and arm 3's own head are scored on the same clips, so most of that cancels in a paired comparison. The under-training gap seen in D-036's training losses (about 0.8 dB) sits just under the 1 dB bound.
    - *Settled (Hannah, 2026-10-27)*: Q1's SNR condition is the paired clip-bootstrap interval excluding 0, with no fixed minimum gain ("more reliable, and fewer magic numbers"), in place of max(1 dB, 2 seed SDs). The seed SD (0.06 dB) is far below the clip sampling noise, so the bootstrap over clips is the interval that matters; the reliability condition still has to hold.
    - *Amendment (Hannah, 2026-10-27 16:45, before any D-037 result; only arm 3's feature cache existed)*: **the 0.5 dB minimum gain is kept** alongside the interval: "we need to have both an effect size and confidence that the effect is real". Q1's SNR condition is therefore: R3's SNR gain over arm 3's own head is at least 0.5 dB **and** its paired clip-bootstrap interval excludes 0; the reliability condition as registered.
  - *Code paths*: the cached F1–F4 are the trunk's own predictions at the same frames (checked against `analyse()` on a few Hillenbrand files); standardisation uses training statistics only; the joint head's cached outputs reproduce `vtl_score_joint.py`'s numbers for arm 3.
- *Code check, seen before the run (2026-10-27 20:40)*: the scorer ran end to end on three throwaway 600-step R3 heads (a smoke test, deleted; their numbers mean nothing for Q1) and arm 3's own joint head. **The registered code-path check passed**: J3 from the cached outputs reproduces D-036's arm 3 exactly (probe 17.9 c/oct, null 11.5, excess +6.4, vowel spread 34 c). Seen in passing: J3's validation SNR 21.05 dB, and the reported-only vowel classification on arm 3's F1–F3 (raw 0.807, + A's speaker length 0.843, + J3's 0.859, Lobanov 0.934). None of the real refits had finished.
- *Result, arm 3's trunk (2026-10-28 00:55; `reports/analysis/d037/d037_arm3.txt`; the other trunks' arms still running, reported later)*. **Q1: not under-trained; item 5 keeps D-036's setup. Q3: the VTL head reads `h` alone.**
  - *Q1*: R3 (refit, 20,000 steps, three seeds) against J3 (arm 3's own head): validation SNR 22.70 against 21.05 dB, gain **+1.65 [+1.29, +2.00]** (passes ≥ 0.5 and > 0); Hillenbrand reliability 0.244 against 0.236, **+0.008 [−0.005, +0.019]** (fails > 0). A longer-trained head is more precise on synthetic data, but not more reliable on real voices: the case the two-part rule was written for. No seed flagged as overfitting (final and minimum validation NLL within 0.007 nats for all six refit seeds).
  - *Q3*: R3c against R3: reliability −0.006 [−0.016, +0.007] (fails); validation SNR +0.02 [−0.04, +0.10] dB. The guards held (probe excess +2.5, rise +1.0; w/m 0.868; vowel spread +1 c), but there is no gain.
  - *Reported*: R3's probe excess is **+1.5 c/oct** (P = 0.33, indistinguishable from its null) against J3's +6.4, but with a larger level bias by length (−21 / −28 / −39 c at 13 / 15.5 / 18 cm, against 0 / −7 / −21): a head refitted on the frozen trunk after training is less f0-dependent and less well calibrated in level. Reliability against A: R3 +0.091 [+0.037, +0.144], R3c +0.085, J3 +0.083. w/m 0.871 / 0.868 / 0.868.
  - *Vowel classification (reported only)*, arm 3's F1–F3: raw 0.807; + the speaker's length from A 0.843, R3 0.851, R3c 0.860, J3 0.859; Lobanov 0.934. Every length estimate helps, the learnt heads a little more than A; Lobanov, which uses the speaker's whole vowel set, stays well ahead.
- *Result, all trunks (2026-10-28 03:33; `reports/analysis/d037/d037.txt`; the decisions above reproduce: Q1 reliability +0.008 [−0.005, +0.020])*:
  - *Arm 1's check (registered: noted beside the decision)*: **on arm 1's trunk Q1 would pass**: R1 against J1, SNR +1.70 [+1.34, +2.04] dB and reliability **+0.017 [+0.002, +0.030]**. Q3 agrees (R1c − R1 reliability −0.008 [−0.019, +0.004]; SNR +0.00). So Q1's "not under-trained" is **not robust across trunks**: the refit's reliability gain is small (+0.008 to +0.017) and its interval sits at zero on arm 3 and just above it on arm 1. On arm 2 too, R2 − J2 is +0.015 [+0.002, +0.027] (SNR +1.38).
  - *Q2 (reported)*: refits on the joint trunks are slightly more reliable than on teacher v3's: R3 − RT +0.015 [+0.001, +0.028], R2 − RT +0.015 [+0.001, +0.028], R1 − RT +0.015 [−0.001, +0.029]; the three joint trunks are alike (R1 − R3 −0.001, R2 − R3 +0.000). Joint training made marginally better length features, whichever head was attached during it.
  - *Refits against joint heads, f0*: every refit has a lower probe excess than its trunk's own head (R3 +1.5 / J3 +6.4; R1 +3.7 / J1 +7.4; R2 −1.1 / J2 +4.6; RT +5.2), with larger level biases by length (about −20 / −30 / −35 c, against the joint heads' 0 / −5 / −15).
  - *Other*: RT (teacher v3's trunk, 20,000 steps on the rebuilt cache) 0.228 against D-035's T 0.224; validation SNR gains of every refit over its joint head +1.1 to +1.7 dB; no seed of any refit flagged as overfitting; all w/m 0.858–0.876.
  - *Reading for item 5* (not a decision): the joint head is not clearly under-trained, but a head **refitted on the frozen trunk after training** is consistently a little more reliable (+0.015 on two of three trunks) and much less f0-dependent, at some cost in level calibration. That is a cheap post-training step worth its own pre-registration for 5b, rather than a change to the joint schedule.
- *Considered, not included (one change at a time)*: early stopping (dropped, Hannah: a fixed realistic length is simpler and matches an end-to-end run); f0 as an explicit head input; a larger head; an adversarial f0 penalty (D-036's f0 guard passed).
- *Cost (estimate)*: feature caching about 45 min of GPU per joint trunk (three) plus a short pass for teacher v3's formant columns; 6 arms × 3 seeds × 20,000 steps of a small MLP, in short GPU bursts or on the CPU. Caching train_T at 58,712 crops takes about 40 min per trunk at D-035's 25 crops/s.

## D-038 (2026-10-28, pre-registered): a VTL → formants cascade

*Status*: split from D-037 (Hannah, 2026-10-27); shares its feature caches. **Registered with Hannah's agreement (2026-10-28), before any Fv or Fi result**: only the control F (three seeds, tube-only and then mixed crops) and the trunk's own heads T0 had been scored. The registered form is the *Registered rule* bullet at the end of this entry; the draft bullets above it are kept as the record of how it got there.

- *Question*: do the formant heads gain from the joint VTL head's length and σ as inputs: a speaker-size prior, expected to help most where formants are weakly observed (F3 / F4)? And is the length easier to use as a log or as an inverse?
- *Trunk*: `d036_arm3` (decides); `d036_arm1` reported.
- *Features*: D-037's caches, plus a fifth part, **cp**: the closed-phase frames of `ptdb_egg_sub` / `arctic_egg_sub` (dev), for the closed-phase measures.
- *Heads*: the trunk's trained formant heads (the F1–F4 cascade with its masks), fine-tuned with the formant loss on D-037's rebuilt cache (58,712 tube crops, about 11 passes) for 20,000 steps (D-037's schedule shape: 300 warm-up steps, cosine), three seeds each. Training and validation loss logged every 500 steps; D-037's overfitting flag applies (final validation formant loss more than 2 % above its own minimum, **proposed**), and a flagged F or Fv seed makes the decision **unreliable** as in D-037.
- *Arms*:
  - **F**: `h` alone, fine-tuned the same way (the control: what fine-tuning on these crops does by itself).
  - **Fv**: with the joint VTL head's **log2 length** and log σ (detached, standardised) appended to each formant head's input, the new weights initialised to 0, so F and Fv start from the same function. Log length is the natural form: the formant axis is logarithmic, and log F = constant − log L, so a linear head can shift its estimate in proportion to log L. (log 1/L = −log L is the same input with its sign flipped, so it is not a separate arm.)
  - **Fi**: as Fv, with **1/L** (cm⁻¹, standardised) in place of log2 length, a scale that is curved against the formant axis. The ablation Hannah asked for: is one form easier to learn?
- *Measures* (every one reported for every formant; paired against F, speaker bootstrap):
  - Hillenbrand dev F1–F4 MAE at the reference frames;
  - closed-phase F1–F3 bias and |error| by sex (cp part);
  - synthetic F1–F4 MAE on train_v2's held-out crops;
  - the invariance probe's formant slopes against f0.
- *Decision rule*: **Fv joins item 5's design** if, against F, the mean of Hillenbrand's F3 and F4 MAE improves (paired interval excluding 0), no formant's Hillenbrand MAE is worse beyond D-036's guard-rails (F1 +4, F2 +9, F3 +20 Hz), and women's closed-phase F1 |error| is not worse by more than 5 Hz. **Fi** is reported against Fv (and against F) and decides only the input's form: if Fi passes and beats Fv on the F3 / F4 mean (paired interval excluding 0), item 5 takes 1/L instead. If both D-037's Q3 and this pass, D-037's loop rule applies.
- *To check before registering*: that Fv's and Fi's predictions equal F's at step 0 (zero-initialised inputs); the fine-tuning seed spread of the F3 / F4 mean on F's three seeds (the interval must be able to exclude 0 at a plausible effect); that the closed-phase features match `add_model.py`'s frames.
- *Checks done (2026-10-28; code `d038_head.py`, `d038_score.py`, `d037_cache.py`'s new `cp` part; only the control F has run, no Fv or Fi result exists)*:
  - *Step 0*: every arm reproduces the trunk's cached F1–F4 on the val part (median |difference| 0.003 cents; < 0.07 % of frames beyond 1 cent, from `h` stored as fp16), and Fv / Fi equal F exactly.
  - *Closed-phase frames*: the `cp` part (PTDB-TUG dev, 9,401 frames, 479 utterances, cache.py's order, reference checked against `cache/emb_v1/gold_ptdb_cp`) agrees with D-036's windowed arm 3 columns in `ptdb_egg_sub.npz` to a median 2.2 / 2.2 / 2.9 Hz (F1–F3; whole files here, windowed there). ARCTIC's three EGG voices are not included.
  - *Validation split*: val holds every clip kind; the training crops are tube clips only. The overfitting flag uses val's tube clips (all clips reported).
  - *Noise floor, F's three seeds on arm 3 (`reports/analysis/d038/noise_floor_F.txt`; seen: the control only)*: no seed flagged. The seeds differ only in crop order and agree closely (Hillenbrand F3 / F4 mean 75.6 / 75.5 / 75.9 Hz), but a paired speaker bootstrap between two seeds of the frame-level F3 / F4 mean spans about ±3 Hz (s1 − s0 +0.9 [−1.6, +4.7]; s2 − s0 −0.4 [−2.2, +1.2]), so an Fv gain needs roughly 2–3 Hz (3–4 %) to exclude 0.
  - *Seen in passing (matters for the design)*: fine-tuning on tube crops alone **costs F3** against the trunk's own heads (T0 − F, Hillenbrand F3 −2.6 [−5.5, −0.6] Hz, i.e. F is worse; synthetic F3 MAE on all clip kinds 60.5 → 65.0 Hz, on tube clips unchanged; closed-phase women's F3 |error| 42.3 → 44.1). F stays the paired control, but the training crops are under discussion with Hannah before registering.
  - *Draft change (Hannah, 2026-10-28, from the F3 cost above; no Fv or Fi result exists)*: fix the cause. The heads fine-tune on a new **mix** part (`d037_cache.py`): teacher v3's training mixture, 400-frame crops from every train_v2 training clip kind (70 %) and its resynthesised VocalSet and Zeroth-Korean sets (30 %, by size), 29,356 crops with fixed seeds, the loss weighted by `w_formant` as in training. The overfitting flag moves to val's clips of every kind. The noise floor is redone on it (`chains/d038_prep.sh`); the tube-crop runs are kept as `runs/d038/arm3_tube` and `noise_floor_F_tube.txt`.
  - *Noise floor on the mix part (2026-10-28 12:24; `reports/analysis/d038/noise_floor_F.txt`; seen: the control only)*: no seed flagged. **The F3 cost is gone**: T0 − F, Hillenbrand F3 −0.0 [−1.4, +1.1] Hz, F3 / F4 mean −0.8 [−2.4, +0.3]; synthetic MAE on every clip kind now improves slightly (F3 60.5 → 59.4, F4 93.0 → 91.0 Hz); women's closed-phase F3 |error| 42.3 → 42.9. Seed against seed (crop order only), the paired F3 / F4 mean is −0.7 [−2.0, +0.1] and −0.2 [−0.4, −0.0]: intervals about ±1 Hz, and two seeds can differ just beyond noise, so arms are compared as seed means and an Fv gain of about 1–2 Hz should be detectable.
