  - *Single-formant-shift probe (added before registering, Hannah 2026-10-28: Fv's main risk is prior over evidence, which Hillenbrand MAE could reward)*: `eval.invariance.shift_clips`: the probe's 18 tracts (6 vowels × 3 lengths) at f0 100 / 158 / 248 Hz, with one of F1–F4 moved by ×0.9 / 0.95 / 1.05 / 1.1 and everything else unchanged (shifts that would come within ×1.10 of a neighbour skipped; 840 clips; ±20 % left few F3 / F4 shifts). Per tract, f0 and moved formant: *follow* = the moved formant's slope of log estimate against log factor (ideal 1); *drag* = the mean |slope| of the other three (ideal 0). Cached as the `shift` part; paired bootstraps over tracts.
    - *Noise floor (`noise_floor_F.txt`; seen: T0 and the control F only)*: follow F1–F4 T0 0.861 / 0.983 / 0.990 / 0.918, F 0.820 / 0.984 / 0.990 / 0.927; drag 0.01–0.03. Seed against seed, the paired intervals are about ±0.003 for F2–F4 follow, ±0.03 for F1 follow (F1 moves least at low f0, where harmonics are sparse), and ±0.005–0.015 for drag. T0 − F is within noise everywhere.
    - *Seen in passing*: the trunk's own VTL head moves when a single formant does (log2 length per log2 factor −0.12 / −0.08 / −0.26 / −0.10 for F1–F4), most for F3, so the joint head reads F3 more than the others.
- *Registered rule (2026-10-28)*. Trunk `d036_arm3` decides; `d036_arm1` is reported as a check (noted beside the decision if it disagrees). Heads fine-tuned on the **mix** part (above), 20,000 steps, three seeds per arm (F, Fv, Fi), the length input from the trunk's own joint VTL head (**J3**: log2 length and log σ, detached, standardised with the mix part's sonorant-frame statistics). Every comparison on seed means (per frame, the mean of the seeds' log2 estimates). A seed is flagged if its final validation formant loss on every clip kind is more than 2 % above its own minimum; a flagged F or Fv seed makes the decision **unreliable** (as D-037). **Fv joins item 5's design** if, against F, all of:
  1. *Primary*: the Hillenbrand dev F3 / F4 mean MAE improves (paired speaker bootstrap, interval excluding 0);
  2. *Guard-rails* (point estimates of the paired differences): Hillenbrand MAE F1 ≤ +4, F2 ≤ +9, F3 ≤ +20, **F4 ≤ +50 Hz** (all about 1 %, 9–15 cents at the dev medians; F4's added at registration so neither of the primary's two formants can be traded for the other); women's closed-phase F1 median |error| ≤ +5 Hz;
  3. *Single-formant shift* (paired over tracts, point estimates): follow slope down by at most 0.02 for F2–F4 and 0.05 for F1 (about 3 and 8 cents per 10 % shift); drag up by at most 0.01 (about 1.7 cents per 10 % shift), every formant;
  4. *f0* (the f0 probe, f0 ≤ 250 Hz, |slope| per tract in cents per octave, paired over tracts, point estimate): up by at most **+5 c/oct**, every formant. Mechanism: J3's own f0 slope is about 18 c/oct, and log F moves one-for-one against log L, so a full use of the input could pass most of it on; seed-to-seed noise is about ±2 (F1), ±0.8 (F2), ±0.15 (F3), ±0.4 (F4) c/oct.
  - **Fi** is reported against F and against Fv and decides only the input's form: item 5 takes 1/L instead of log2 L if Fi passes all four and beats Fv on the F3 / F4 mean (paired interval excluding 0).
  - If both D-037's Q3 and this rule pass, D-037's loop rule applies (D-037's Q3 failed, so it cannot).
  - Everything else is reported: every formant's MAE, closed-phase bias and |error| by sex, synthetic MAE by clip kind, the probe's error by length, the shift probe's per-formant slopes and the trunk VTL head's response.
  - *Cost*: 9 heads per trunk, about 20 min each, three at a time on the GPU; then the scoring. `chains/d038.sh`.
- **Result (2026-10-28 17:55; `reports/analysis/d038/d038_arm3.txt`, `d038_arm1.txt`): Fv does not join item 5; neither trunk is unreliable (no seed flagged).**
  - *Arm 3 (decides)*: Fv − F, Hillenbrand F3 / F4 mean −0.02 [−0.04, +0.00] Hz; every difference is a few hundredths of a hertz, and every guard-rail, shift and f0 criterion passes. Fi matches Fv to the hundredth (Fi − Fv 0.00), so the form question is moot.
  - *Arm 1 (check, agrees)*: Fv − F, F3 / F4 mean −0.19 [−0.54, +0.01], F3 −0.07 [−0.20, −0.00]; the guard-rails, shift and f0 criteria pass. The primary fails here too, and its point estimate is a tenth of the 1–2 Hz the noise floor could detect.
  - *Reading*: a head given the trunk's own length estimate learns almost nothing from it. The length is already in h (the joint head reads it from h linearly), so a zero-initialised extra input has nothing new to add. The cascade's question for item 5 is then not "is length an input" but whether a VTL head trained jointly from scratch changes h itself, which is D-040's 5b.
  - *Seen in passing, the control's own cost on arm 1*: T0 − F, Hillenbrand F4 −2.9 [−6.2, −0.3] Hz and F1's f0 slope −2.8 [−5.8, −0.3] c/oct; i.e. fine-tuning the heads on the mix part, with no new input, costs arm 1 about 3 Hz on F4 and adds about 3 c/oct of F1 f0 leakage. On arm 3 both are within noise (F4 −1.2 [−3.7, +0.4]). It does not affect this decision (every arm is compared with F), but a later head-only refit should be checked against the trunk's own heads (T0), not only against a refit control.

## D-039 (2026-10-28, pre-registered; exploratory, reported only): VTL heads over teacher and Parakeet features together

*Status*: registered with Hannah's agreement on the arms (2026-10-28, about 01:20), before anything ran. Exploratory: **it decides nothing**; a result worth acting on gets its own pre-registered entry. Cheap because every feature is already cached (D-035).

- *Question*: D-035's Parakeet-layer-5 head (P) was the more reliable on Hillenbrand (P − T +0.073 [+0.009, +0.133]) but read women's tracts relatively longer (w/m 0.052 above A's) and failed the f0 guard, like the teacher head (T). Does a head reading both do better than either, and how should they be combined?
- *Data* (D-035's caches, unchanged): training crops = cache_heads.py's tube crops with length not drawn from f0 (8,263; T features from `cache/vtl/train_T`, P from `cache/heads_l3_6/emb.npy` layer 5), sonorant frames; validation, probe and Hillenbrand dev as D-035 (`cache/vtl/{val,probe,hb}`, `cache/emb_v1`).
- *Alignment*: fusion runs at the teacher's 10 ms frames, each taking the Parakeet frame that covers it, with D-035's conventions: in crops, encoder frame e ↔ teacher frame 8e − 4 (e ≥ 1); in whole files (probe, Hillenbrand), e ↔ 8e (Hillenbrand's Parakeet features are cached at its reference frames).
- *Arms* (trained arms: D-035's recipe, AdamW 1e-3, wd 0.01, 300 warm-up steps, cosine over 4,000 steps, batch 32 crops, Gaussian NLL; three seeds each, seed k paired with D-035's T and P seed k where they are used):
  1. **T**: D-035's teacher heads (reused, not retrained).
  2. **P**: D-035's Parakeet heads (reused), read at Parakeet's own frames as in D-035.
  3. **E, early fusion**: one head on both inputs. Each source has its own LayerNorm; then T (128) ‖ P (1024) ‖ two presence indicators → 256 → GELU → 256 → GELU → mean, log variance.
  4. **ED, early fusion with source dropout**: as E; per crop, 50 % both sources, 25 % T only, 25 % P only. A dropped source is set to 0 after its LayerNorm and its indicator to 0; no rescaling. Evaluation always sees both.
  5. **F, penultimate fusion** (Hannah): D-035's T and P heads, frozen, without their output layers (their 256-d activations after the second GELU), concatenated with the two indicators → 256 → GELU → mean, log variance. Only the new layers train.
  6. **FD, penultimate fusion with source dropout** (Hannah: the cache makes it cheap): as F with ED's dropout on the frozen sub-heads' activations.
  7. **W, training-free late fusion**: per frame, the precision-weighted mean of T's and P's seed-mean predictions (μ = Σ μᵢ/σᵢ² / Σ 1/σᵢ², σ² = 1 / Σ 1/σᵢ²). It treats the two as independent, so its σ is overconfident; only its weights matter for the per-item estimate.
- *Measures* (D-035 / D-036's, from one script): synthetic validation per-clip |error| by kind, validation NLL and SNR on tube clips; the probe's median |slope| at f0 ≤ 250 Hz with D-036's shuffle null and the excess; vowel spread; bias by length; Hillenbrand dev reliability, w/m, within-speaker SD, group medians, r(length, f0) within sex; paired speaker bootstrap (2,000) of every arm against T, against P and against A. Also the vowel classification of D-037 (teacher v3's F1–F3, normalised by each arm's speaker length).
- *What would be interesting* (stated now, decides nothing): a fused arm more reliable than P with w/m inside [0.85, 0.92] and a lower probe excess than either alone; whether source dropout moves the fused arms towards P's real-voice behaviour; whether a trained fusion (E, F) ends up weighting T, the better source on synthetic data, at P's expense, which W cannot do.
- *Cost*: four trained arms × 3 seeds × 4,000 steps of a small MLP on the GPU (minutes each), alongside D-037's caching.
- *Code check*: the scorer reproduces D-035's T and P exactly (reliability 0.224 / 0.297, probe 24.3 / 142.1 c/oct), so the alignment and seed means are right.
- *Result (2026-10-28 02:36; `reports/analysis/d039/d039.txt`; exploratory, decides nothing)*:

  | arm | reliability | − T | − P | w/m | probe excess (c/oct) | vowel spread | within-speaker SD | val SNR |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | T | 0.224 | | | 0.860 | +9.4 | 46 c | 118 c | 21.3 dB |
  | P | 0.297 | +0.073 [+0.010, +0.140] | | 0.915 | +37.6 (slope 142) | 65 c | 106 c | 13.7 dB |
  | E | 0.261 | +0.037 [+0.005, +0.071] | −0.036 [−0.085, +0.015] | 0.880 | +5.7 | 48 c | 107 c | 20.5 dB |
  | ED | 0.266 | +0.042 [+0.017, +0.065] | −0.031 [−0.086, +0.022] | 0.868 | +18.1 | 46 c | 106 c | 20.8 dB |
  | **F** | **0.281** | **+0.057 [+0.025, +0.083]** | −0.017 [−0.066, +0.028] | 0.881 | **+3.1** (P = 0.29) | **32 c** | **99 c** | 20.4 dB |
  | FD | 0.239 | +0.015 [−0.003, +0.033] | −0.058 [−0.111, −0.009] | 0.878 | +7.5 | 40 c | 105 c | 20.5 dB |
  | W | 0.249 | +0.025 [+0.004, +0.044] | −0.048 [−0.100, +0.001] | 0.883 | +7.7 | 50 c | 101 c | 19.9 dB |

  - Every fused arm sits between T and P on reliability; none beats P (A 0.153). Every fused arm keeps w/m inside [0.85, 0.92] and an f0 excess near T's or below it, against P's +37.6 and its large level bias on the synthetic probe (−178 / −432 / −625 c at 13 / 15.5 / 18 cm).
  - **F, Hannah's penultimate fusion, is the most interesting**: reliability within noise of P (−0.017 [−0.066, +0.028]) and well above T (+0.057), with the lowest f0 excess (+3.1, indistinguishable from its null), vowel spread (32 c) and within-speaker SD (99 c). Its level calibration on the synthetic probe is worse than T's (−47 / −117 / −152 c). For scale, D-037's joint head J3 had reliability 0.236 and excess +6.4.
  - Source dropout did not help: ED is about E on reliability with a much larger f0 excess (+18.1); FD is below F on reliability (0.239).
  - The trained fusions did not simply collapse onto T (all are more reliable than T), but none kept all of P's real-voice advantage.
  - *Vowel classification* (teacher v3's F1–F3; reported): raw 0.804; normalised by A 0.844, T 0.862, P 0.836, E 0.848, ED 0.860, F 0.851, FD 0.854, W 0.847; Lobanov 0.933. Which length normalises vowels best does not follow reliability (T best, P worst).
  - *Caution*: seven arms were compared, and F is the best of four trained fusions on one dev set, so its lead is partly selection. Anything acted on needs its own pre-registered entry with fresh seeds (e.g. F as the offline labeller's length output, as H5×6 is for F1).

## D-040 (2026-10-28, pre-registered): roadmap items 5a and 5b, the full-size control and the joint VTL head from scratch

*Status*: **decided 2026-10-29: 5b (rA + joint VTL head) is teacher v3.1** (seed 2, `d040_5b_s2_b20k`, Hannah 2026-10-29; tag `teacher-v3.1`). **Registered with Hannah's agreement (2026-10-28), before any 5a or 5b run**. Seen beforehand: the head-optimiser check, D-041, the replica check and a 60-step smoke run of the 5b command (none of them a 5b result). The registered form is the *Registered* bullet at the end of this entry. Item 5 is split so each addition has its own control (Hannah, 2026-10-28). If D-038 adopts a VTL → formants cascade, it is a further arm on top of 5b, in its own entry (one change at a time).

- *Question*: trained from scratch with D-031's adopted recipe, does adding D-036's joint VTL head change the formants, f0 or bandwidths, and does its length beat A, as it did in D-036's decay branch?
- *Arms* (full size, teacher v3's data and command as D-031's rA: train_v2 + resynthesised VocalSet and Zeroth-Korean, mixed crops 32–400, `muon_adan` Muon 2e-3 / Adan ×5 / wd 0.007, warm-up 1,000, 16,000 constant + 4,000 decay):
  - **5a, control**: no VTL head. Seed 1 is `d031_rA_b20k`, already trained (its config has today's heads: no observability head, no VTL head), if a short replica check shows the current code reproduces its first steps bit for bit (D-027's replica check); otherwise seed 1 is retrained. **Seed 2 is new.**
  - **5b, + joint VTL head**: as 5a, seeds 1 and 2, with D-036's head (weight 0.0125, own warm-up 300, gate 0 for 300 steps then a ramp over 700; fresh head, `vtl_seed` 0; no formant inputs, D-037).
  - **5b-R, reported**: D-037's post-training refit on each 5b trunk (frozen; D-037's recipe, 20,000 steps, three head seeds). Cheap, and D-037 suggests it is a little more reliable and much less f0-dependent.
- *Open points before registering*:
  1. *The head's schedule from scratch*. In D-036 the gate and the head's own warm-up and cosine counted from the branch. From scratch, `_vtl_lr` and `_vtl_gate` count from step 0: the head's rate decays by cosine over all 20,000 steps while the model's is constant until 16,000, and the gate opens while the trunk is still untrained. Options: as is; or open the gate and start the head's warm-up at the model's warm-up end (step 1,000); or hold the head's rate with the model's and decay it in the last 4,000 steps.
  2. *The head's optimiser*. D-036's head had AdamW 1e-3; under `muon_adan` it would join Adan's group at that group's rate (×5) unless set otherwise. The check: which group it lands in and at what rate.
  3. *Seeds*: two per arm, paired by seed (training data stream and model initialisation shared within a seed; only the head differs). The f0 margin in D-036 was about one seed's spread, so the decision uses seed means.
- *Measures*: as D-036. Formants, f0, bandwidths against 5a (synthetic in SE units; dev guard-rails Hillenbrand F1 ≤ +4, F2 ≤ +9, F3 ≤ +20 Hz, PTDB-TUG RPA ≥ −1.0, women's closed phase ≤ +5); length (`vtl_score_joint.py`): Hillenbrand reliability against A, w/m, probe excess over its shuffle null, vowel spread, bias by length. Item 5's notes: F3 and the f0 excess per seed. Also the single-formant-shift probe (D-038) for every arm, reported.
- *Decision rule (proposed)*: 5b becomes item 5's model if, on the seed means against 5a, D-036's three criteria hold: no formant cost; reliability above A's (paired interval excluding 0) with w/m in [0.85, 0.92]; probe excess ≤ 10 c/oct. Otherwise 5a is teacher v4 and A stays the length output. 5b-R is reported against 5b; adopting the refit step would need its own entry.
- *Cost*: about 8 h per run: 5a seed 2, 5b seeds 1 and 2 (and 5a seed 1 if the replica check fails), so 24–32 h of GPU, plus minutes for 5b-R.
- *Agreed since the draft (Hannah, 2026-10-28)*: the head follows the model's schedule (gate opens and the head's warm-up starts at step 1,000, the end of the model's warm-up; the gate ramps over 700 steps; the head's rate is held through the constant phase and decays with the model's over the last 4,000 steps). The head's optimiser is settled by the check below. D-034's evidence was "no gain" for Muon + Adan on a frozen-feature head, not harm, and separate recipes for trunk and head in joint training have never been tested, so one recipe throughout is a real option.
- *Code fix needed before 5b, whatever the check decides*: under `muon_adan`, `muon_split` does not exclude `vtl_head` (it is not in `HEAD_MODULES`), so a from-scratch run would put the head's linear weights in Muon and the rest in Adan, and the trainer would then add the same parameters again as the VTL group. D-036 ran on AdamW, where `_optimizer` excludes the head, so it never hit this. The fix: exclude `vtl_head.` from both Muon and the rest in the `muon` / `muon_adan` branch, so the head is only ever in its own group, with the optimiser this check picks. *Done (2026-10-28)*: in fact the trainer refused a VTL head under Muon (a guard, so 5b would have failed at start rather than double-counted). `_optimizer` now leaves `vtl_head.` out of both Muon and the rest; `_add_vtl_group` places the group by `vtl_optimizer`: `rest` (the non-Muon optimiser at `rest_lr_scale`, which for AdamW runs is D-036's setup exactly) or `adamw` (its own AdamW at `vtl_lr` / `vtl_weight_decay`, a third optimiser under Muon); the guard now admits `muon` / `muon_adan`. Test `test_vtl_head_under_muon_adan` (every parameter in exactly one group, the rates, a branch that trains, checkpoints and resumes); D-036's and the Muon tests still pass.
- *Check, registered 2026-10-28 before it ran (Hannah: "determine and register the formal acceptance parameters")*: **the head's optimiser in joint training** (5b), from a frozen-trunk refit.
  - *Arms*: on `d036_arm3`'s frozen trunk (D-037's cache, its tube-crop train part), D-037's refit recipe (20,000 steps, 300 warm-up, cosine, batch 32, three seeds): **R3**, AdamW 1e-3 / wd 0.01 (D-037's existing seeds, reused unchanged), against **R3a**, Adan at the rate and decay a head would get in rA's Adan group (1e-2 = 2e-3 × `rest_lr_scale` 5, wd 0.007; `VA_OPT=adan` in `d037_head.py`). One change: the optimiser. A 1,000-step smoke run of R3a was stable (validation 21.6 dB at step 1,000); nothing else had been seen.
  - *Measures*: D-037's (`d037_score.py`, seed means): Hillenbrand dev reliability, w/m and within-speaker SD with the paired speaker bootstrap; probe excess over its shuffle null; validation SNR with the paired clip bootstrap; overfitting flags.
  - *Rule*: **one recipe throughout (the VTL group in Adan at the rest group's rate and decay)** if R3a against R3 is **not worse**: reliability's paired interval lower bound above **−0.02** (a non-inferiority margin: about the size of D-037's refit gains, and about four head-seed SDs, D-035), probe excess ≤ R3's + 3 c/oct and ≤ 10, w/m in [0.85, 0.92], and no R3a seed flagged. Otherwise **route A**: the VTL group keeps AdamW 1e-3 / wd 0.01 inside the rA run. Validation SNR is reported only.
  - *Limits*: a frozen trunk is not joint training; this picks the default with the cheapest evidence there is, and 5b's own measures still decide whether the head joins.
  - *Result (2026-10-28 14:20; `reports/analysis/d040/check_optimizer.txt`)*: **route A: the VTL head keeps AdamW 1e-3 / wd 0.01 inside the rA run** (`vtl_optimizer="adamw"`, `vtl_lr=1e-3`, `vtl_weight_decay=0.01`).
    - Reliability R3a − R3 −0.006 [−0.018, +0.006]: passes the non-inferiority margin (lower bound above −0.02), only just.
    - **Probe excess R3a +8.9 c/oct against R3's +1.5** (P(null ≥ observed) 0.002 against 0.33): fails "≤ R3's + 3" (it passes ≤ 10). This is the deciding criterion.
    - w/m 0.865 (in range); no seed flagged; validation SNR R3a 22.87 against R3 22.70 dB (reported).
    - *Reading*: the same pattern as D-034's Muon + Adan head: a better fit on synthetic data (+0.17 dB) with nothing gained on real voices, here with a clear rise in f0 dependence. A faster optimiser seems to pick up the f0 cue more, which also fits D-037's observation that heads fitted gently on a frozen trunk are the least f0-dependent.
- *5a replica check (2026-10-28, commit `a2528e4`)*: rA's exact command (D-031's `trunk rA`) rerun on the current code reproduces `d031_rA`'s log at steps 50 and 100 to every digit (loss 11.59790283203125 / 10.192691650390625, gradient norm 7.28676513671875 / 4.621672058105469). Stopped at step 100 (sharing the GPU with D-038). **`d031_rA_b20k` stands as 5a's seed 1**; only seed 2 is new.
- *Code fix before registering (2026-10-28)*: a decay branch sets `decay_start`, from which the head's warm-up and gate counted (D-036's head was added at its branch), so a head trained from step 0 would have lost its rate and closed its gate for the first 1,000 steps of the decay branch. New field `vtl_start` (0 for 5b; `branch_config` copies it); `None` keeps D-036's behaviour. Tests pass; a 60-step smoke run of the 5b command trained the head under `muon_adan` and logged its loss.
- *Registered (2026-10-28)*:
  - *Runs*, each D-031's rA path exactly (`trunk` to 16,000 constant, then the 4,000-step decay branch; `chains/d040.sh`): **5a seed 2** (`seed=2`); **5b seeds 1 and 2**, adding `vtl=new vtl_start=0 vtl_schedule=model vtl_detach=1000 vtl_warmup=300 vtl_gate_ramp=700 vtl_optimizer=adamw vtl_lr=0.001 vtl_weight_decay=0.01 vtl_seed=0` (loss weight 0.0125). 5a seed 1 = `d031_rA_b20k` (replica check). Order: 5b seed 1 (pairs with the existing 5a seed 1), 5a seed 2, 5b seed 2.
  - *Measures*: as the draft (D-036's formant, f0 and bandwidth set against 5a; `vtl_score_joint.py`'s length set; the single-formant-shift and f0 probes for every arm, reported). The scoring script is written and checked on 5a before any 5b result is seen.
  - *Rule*: on seed means against 5a (seeds paired), **5b becomes item 5's model** if D-036's three criteria hold: (1) no formant cost (synthetic F1–F4 no worse than +2 SE, RPA not below −0.1; dev guard-rails Hillenbrand F1 ≤ +4, F2 ≤ +9, F3 ≤ +20 Hz, PTDB-TUG RPA ≥ −1.0, women's closed phase ≤ +5 Hz); (2) Hillenbrand reliability above A's (paired interval excluding 0) with w/m in [0.85, 0.92]; (3) probe excess ≤ 10 c/oct. Otherwise 5a is teacher v4 and A stays the length output. Per seed, F3 and the f0 excess are reported beside the decision (D-036's thin margins), and if the two seeds disagree on any criterion the decision is flagged **not robust to the seed**.
  - *Reported*: 5b-R (D-037's refit on each 5b trunk) against 5b; a decision to adopt it would need its own entry.
- *Naming (Hannah, 2026-10-28, before any run; no change to the rule)*: item 5's model is **teacher v3.1**, not v4. Major versions follow milestones and training data (v1–v3: M1–M3); item 5 changes the recipe and heads on teacher v3's data inside M3, so it is a minor version, and v4 is kept for M4's teacher (re-tracked labels). Where this entry says "teacher v4", read v3.1; a second-seed ensemble would be labeller v3.1, and 5c, if it changes the training data inside M3, v3.2.
- *Amendment, logging only (Hannah, 2026-10-28 18:40; seen: 5b seed 1's training log to step 3,200, no scored result)*: the VTL head had a training loss (`log.jsonl` only) but no validation measure. `teacher.evaluate` now adds `vtl_mae_pct`, `vtl_bias_pct`, `vtl_nll` and `vtl_cover1` (share within ±1σ, ideal 0.683) on the eval set's labelled sonorant frames (24,317), and `train.log` prints the vtl loss. The validation sets get the vtl label, which adds no random draws, so their crops are unchanged; training is unchanged. 5b seed 1 (started before the change) is backfilled from its checkpoints by `scripts/analysis/vtl_val_backfill.py` (`val_vtl.jsonl`); 5a seed 2 and 5b seed 2 log it directly. Reported only; the rule is unchanged. *Seen while checking*: the training VTL loss's 50-step noise (SD about 0.2 nats) does not move with the other losses (step-to-step correlations −0.09 to +0.09), so it is batch sampling of the few labelled frames, not interference; validation falls steadily (MAE 4.1 % at step 2,000, 2.6 % at 3,000).
- *Amendment (Hannah, 2026-10-28, before any 5a or 5b run had produced a checkpoint)*: **5a-R**, D-037's refit (same recipe and seeds as 5b-R) on each 5a trunk (seed 1 = `d031_rA_b20k`, seed 2 = 5a seed 2), reported only. **5b-R − 5a-R**, paired by seed with the speaker bootstrap, answers Hannah's question: does training from scratch with the VTL head and its gradient build better length features, apart from how well the joint head reads them (D-037's Q2 at full training: +0.015 for a decay branch)? 5b against 5a on every formant measure answers the formant half. Both decide nothing. If D-038 adopts the VTL → formants cascade, it is added on top of the winner in its own entry.
- *Scoring queued (2026-10-29 16:40; training done at 16:11; no 5b measure seen: only the training logs' "training done")*. `chains/d040_score.sh`: **stage A**, 5a only (closed phase and windowed paired measures for 5a seed 2, 5a-R on 5a seed 2, the shift part of 5a seed 1's cache, and `decide_d040.py --check` on the two 5a seeds with D-036's length json: the registered "checked on 5a before any 5b result"); **stage B** waits for a hand-written `runs/d040/score_checked` after the check is read, then 5b seed 1's VTL validation backfill, 5b's closed phase and paired measures (5a seed 1 vs 5b seed 1, 5a seed 2 vs 5b seed 2), `vtl_score_joint.py` with seed means, 5b-R refits, the refit report (5b-R against its joint head J and against 5a-R, per seed), D-041's f0 / shift probes for every arm (5b against the 5a seeds), and `decide_d040.py`. The rule is implemented as registered (seed means; per-seed criteria; "not robust to the seed" if they disagree). Scorer additions, defaults unchanged: `vtl_score_joint.py` seed means (`VA_SEED_MEANS`), `d037_score.py` extra pairs (`VA_PAIRS`), `d041_invariance.py` reference group prefix (`VA_REF_PREFIX`) and its rA decision line only when rA is scored.
  - *Stage A checked (17:45)*: `decide_d040.py --check` on 5a seed 2 against seed 1 (`reports/analysis/d040/check_5a.txt`); every number it printed was recomputed by hand from its source (the two runs' last `log.jsonl` evals, the paired w400 file, `decide_d036.txt`'s arm 1) and matched. Then the marker was written and stage B ran.
- *Result (2026-10-29 20:19; `reports/analysis/d040/decide_d040.txt`, `vtl_joint.txt`, `refits.txt`, `invariance.txt`)*: **5b becomes item 5's model: teacher v3.1 is rA with the joint VTL head.** All three criteria hold on the seed means and on each seed, so the decision is robust to the seed.
  - (1) No formant cost. Seed means against 5a: synthetic F1–F4 +0.5 / −0.2 / +0.4 / +0.9 SE, RPA −0.02 point; dev Hillenbrand F1 / F2 / F3 +0.9 / −1.4 / +0.9 Hz, PTDB-TUG RPA −0.4, women's closed-phase F1 +0.5 Hz. Per seed, F3 is +0.6 SE / +3.0 Hz and +0.2 SE / −1.1 Hz (D-036's small F3 cost does not recur). The closest margin is seed 2's PTDB-TUG RPA, −0.8 against −1.0.
  - (2) Length beats A: reliability − A on the seed mean is [+0.050, +0.130], with w/m 0.865 (seeds [+0.036, +0.113], 0.869 and [+0.061, +0.150], 0.860).
  - (3) The f0 guard holds by a wide margin: probe excess +0.5 and +0.7 c/oct (seed mean +0.6), against D-036's +4.8 to +7.3 for the joint head on a decay branch. Trained from scratch with the trunk, the joint head barely reads f0.
  - *Reported, deciding nothing*:
    - **Formant f0 invariance (D-041's probe, 5b against the mean of the two 5a seeds)**: **F1's |slope| against f0 rises**: seed 1 +8.7 c/oct [+1.4, +15.9], beyond both 5a seeds, over D-041's +5 bound (the probe's "worse" flag); seed 2 +6.4 [−0.8, +14.9], beyond both but with an interval including 0. The tract medians overlap (5a 22.7 / 24.7, 5b 26.7 / 22.2 c/oct), and the reference is two seeds, not D-041's three. F2–F4's f0 slopes and every shift follow / drag measure are inside their bounds. Untested, so a question for item 7's invariance suite: whether the VTL gradient pulls F1 toward length cues that co-vary with f0 (half the synthetic lengths are drawn from f0, `p_length_from_f0` 0.5).
    - **5b-R against 5a-R (Hannah's question: does joint training build better length features?)**: probably yes, modestly. Reliability +0.012 [−0.024, +0.053] (seed 1) and +0.038 [+0.007, +0.069] (seed 2); w/m +0.023 and +0.021; within-speaker SD −5 and −10 cents. Same direction on both seeds; only seed 2's reliability interval excludes 0.
    - **5b-R against 5b's own joint head**: no consistent gain (reliability +0.014 [−0.011, +0.038] and −0.015 [−0.043, +0.016]); the refits' probe excess (−1.3 and +4.1) is no better than the joint heads' (+0.4, +0.8). Nothing argues for a refit step.
    - Hillenbrand lengths (joint heads, seeds 1 / 2): men 17.0 / 17.3 cm, women 14.8 / 14.9, children 13.9 / 14.0. Vowel classification from the trunk's own F1–F3 plus log length: 0.857 / 0.870 (raw 0.806 / 0.818, A 0.844 / 0.848, Lobanov 0.938 / 0.933).
  - *Next*: which seed becomes the released teacher v3.1 (tagged; teacher v3 was a single seed) is Hannah's call: seed 2 has the better length numbers and the smaller F1 f0 slope, seed 1 the better PTDB-TUG RPA. Then 5c on 5b.

## D-041 (2026-10-28, pre-registered): is D-031's Muon + Adan recipe less invariant than teacher v3's AdamW?

*Status*: registered with Hannah's agreement before anything ran. Prompted by D-040's check (an Adan VTL head on a frozen trunk: f0 excess +8.9 against AdamW's +1.5 c/oct) and D-034 (a Muon + Adan formant head: lower training loss, no real-voice gain). D-031 adopted rA mostly on synthetic SE units (better F2 and F4; real voices about neutral), and no one has compared the recipes' invariance. Hannah: if Muon + Adan is less invariant, the recipe decision is re-evaluated.

- *Models* (existing checkpoints, nothing trained): teacher v3's three AdamW seeds (`conformer_v2r2_d16k`, `_s1_d16k`, `_s2_d16k`; seeds 0, 1, 2) against **rA** (`d031_rA_b20k`, seed 1, the adopted recipe). Reported, deciding nothing: **rB** and **rC** (2× and 4× rA's rates, seed 1), as a dose-response check.
- *Measures* (each model's own formant heads, whole clips, `d037_cache.py`'s probe and shift parts; `d041_invariance.py`): per formant F1–F4, the f0 probe's |slope| against log2 f0 at f0 ≤ 250 Hz in cents per octave (per tract); the single-formant-shift probe's follow and drag slopes (D-038). Paired bootstraps over tracts (2,000) of rA minus the per-tract mean of teacher v3's three seeds.
- *Rule*: rA is **less invariant** if, for any formant, any of D-038's thresholds is exceeded *and* the paired interval excludes 0 *and* rA lies beyond all three teacher v3 seeds (so a difference within AdamW's own seed spread does not count):
  - f0 |slope| up by more than **+5 c/oct**;
  - follow down by more than **0.02** (F2–F4) or **0.05** (F1);
  - drag up by more than **0.01**.
- *Then*: if rA is less invariant, item 5 pauses before 5b and a re-evaluation entry compares the recipes with seed replicates (5a's seed 2, rA's second seed, is the first), the invariance thresholds as guard-rails beside D-031's synthetic rule. Otherwise D-040 goes ahead on rA as agreed (route A).
- *Limits*: one rA seed against three AdamW seeds; the "beyond all three" condition guards against reading seed noise as a recipe effect, and makes the rule conservative (it can miss a real but small difference).
- *Result (2026-10-28 14:38; `reports/analysis/d041/d041.txt`)*: **rA is not less invariant than teacher v3's AdamW seeds; D-040 goes ahead on rA (route A).** No criterion was crossed for any formant.
  - f0 |slope| (median over tracts, F1–F4): rA 22.7 / 5.6 / 1.8 / 5.1 c/oct against the AdamW seeds' 23.4–24.6 / 4.8–8.7 / 3.9–4.9 / 3.0–4.1. Paired against the AdamW mean, rA is *lower* on F1 and F3 (intervals below 0) and within noise on F2 and F4.
  - Shift probe: everything within the bounds. The closest: **rA's F1 follow 0.784 against 0.840–0.850** (−0.036 [−0.081, +0.008], beyond all three seeds but inside the 0.05 bound with its interval spanning 0); F3 drag +0.009 [−0.014, +0.047] (bound 0.01).
  - Dose response (reported): rB and rC are within noise of AdamW too, with no trend with the rate (rB's F1 follow 0.850, rC's 0.860), so the F1 follow dip is rA's seed or chance rather than a recipe effect. Item 5's seed replicate (5a seed 2) shows which.
  - *Reading*: the f0 cost of Muon + Adan is specific to small heads on frozen features (D-034, D-040's check), not to the whole model, whose features and heads train together. D-031's adoption stands; the VTL head keeps AdamW inside rA (D-040's check).

## D-042 (2026-10-28, draft, not registered): is Adan too hot for a head on frozen features, or just worse?

*On hold (Hannah, 2026-10-28 23:00): committed as a draft; it is registered and run only after roadmap item 8's low-effort probes (the bias triangulation: D-043, the inverse-filtering test, the domain discriminator). Its two checks before registering still apply.*

Hannah: maybe Muon + Adan is not worse on frozen features, and R3a (D-040's optimiser check) only had too high a rate or too short a warm-up. R3a fitted synthetic data slightly better than AdamW (validation 22.87 against 22.70 dB) and was non-inferior on reliability; what failed was f0 dependence (probe excess +8.9 against +1.5 c/oct). Its rate, 1e-2, is rA's Adan group's (2e-3 × `rest_lr_scale` 5). That ratio was carried over from D-030's small model and scaled with Muon in D-031, never chosen for heads, and D-031 never scored f0 invariance. Frozen features also cannot adjust their scale to a head's rate as a trunk does. D-041 found no f0 cost of rA end to end, so the question is specific to heads on frozen features.

- *Why not warm-up shape*: the usual reason for warm-up is the second-moment estimate's start-up, about 2 / (1 − β₂) steps (Ma & Yarats 2021). `adan_pytorch`'s second moment has β ≈ 0.99, with bias correction, so that is about 200 steps, and R3a had 300. A too-high peak would act through the whole constant phase, not the first few hundred steps. So the peak and the warm-up's length are tested; the warm-up's shape is a follow-up only if length matters (cosine and polynomial warm-ups act like a somewhat longer linear one; exponential is steepest near the peak, the opposite of what is wanted).
- *Arms*: on `d036_arm3`'s frozen trunk (D-037's cache, its tube-crop train part), D-037's refit recipe (20,000 steps, cosine after warm-up, batch 32, seeds s0–s2), each arm one change from R3a:
  - **R3** (AdamW 1e-3, wd 0.01, 300 warm-up) and **R3a** (Adan 1e-2, wd 0.007, 300 warm-up): D-040's existing seeds, reused unchanged.
  - **Ad3**: Adan 3e-3, otherwise R3a.
  - **Ad1**: Adan 1e-3, otherwise R3a.
  - **AdW**: Adan 1e-2 with a 2,000-step linear warm-up, otherwise R3a.
  - Weight decay stays 0.007 in every Adan arm, so the rate is the one change (the decay's span lengthens as the rate falls; D-031 found no effect of decay at full size).
- *Measures*: D-040's check's, with `d037_score.py` (seed means): Hillenbrand dev reliability with the paired speaker bootstrap, w/m, within-speaker SD; probe excess over its shuffle null; validation SNR (paired clip bootstrap); overfitting flags. Each arm against R3, and the three new arms against R3a.
- *Rule*: an arm **passes** under D-040's check's criteria against R3: reliability's paired lower bound above −0.02; probe excess ≤ R3's + 3 c/oct and ≤ 10; w/m in [0.85, 0.92]; no seed flagged. Then:
  - Ad3 or Ad1 passes and AdW does not → **the peak rate**. The candidate is the passing arm with the higher rate (the smaller change from rA's group).
  - AdW passes and neither lower peak does → **early-phase effect**. The warm-up's shape gets one follow-up arm (cosine over 2,000 steps), registered separately.
  - Both kinds pass → the peak; AdW is reported.
  - None passes → **Adan is worse for frozen heads at these rates**; route A stands, and the end-to-end follow-up in `considering.md` is dropped.
- *Confirmation (exploratory sweep, so a winner is not trusted on its own seeds)*: the candidate and R3 rerun on fresh seeds s3–s5, scored the same way. A pass there is D-042's result; anything else is reported as not confirmed. Neither outcome changes D-040 (registered and running).
- *Before registering*:
  1. *Noise floor*: score R3's and R3a's seeds one by one (probe excess per seed), to check the 3 c/oct margin is above seed-to-seed noise. Today the probe is computed on seed means only, so it is unknown. If the noise is comparable, the margin or the number of seeds changes before registering.
  2. *Code path*: `d037_head.py` takes `VA_LR` and `VA_WARM` (default to today's values, so R3 and R3a reproduce), and `d037_score.py` learns the new arms and pairs. A 1,000-step smoke run of AdW and Ad1.
- *Cost*: 9 heads (3 arms × 3 seeds), about 20 min each, three at a time beside D-040; then 6 for the confirmation.
- *Limits*: frozen features are not joint training, and these are VTL heads, not formant heads. Whether a lower head rate helps end to end is the conditional follow-up in `considering.md` ("Head rate inside rA").

## D-043 (2026-10-28, pre-registered): the closed-phase reference's own bias over real voices' conditions

Hannah, after a discussion of the three "truths" (generator, reference, real): before attributing women's F1 overread to the models, measure the reference on audio whose resonances are known, across the conditions where the overread lives. D-026's render check did this at two points only (men f0 110, women f0 210; open quotient 0.5; complete closure): women +3.0 / −0.1 / +0.7 Hz, men +1.3 / +2.9 / +2.4 (with feedback), "unbiased on our physics". PTDB-TUG dev women's frames span f0 173–324 Hz (10th–90th percentile, median 220) and EGG open quotient 0.45–0.69 (median 0.57); men 91–147 Hz and 0.42–0.63. At high f0 and open quotient the closed phase is a few milliseconds, which is where covariance LPC has least data, and incomplete closure (a gap) leaves the subglottal coupling in the "closed" phase.

- *Grid* (`scripts/analysis/cp_ref_grid.py`, CPU, about 10 min): D-026's two tract sizes (men 17.5 cm, women 15 cm shapes) × f0 100 / 140 / 180 / 220 / 270 / 330 Hz × open quotient 0.45 / 0.57 / 0.70 × closed-phase gap 0 / 0.05 / 0.10 of the peak area × Story's 10 vowels; 1 s static; the interactive tube renderer with the load's feedback (D-026's "D"), Rosenberg area pulse. At gap 0 also without feedback ("C"). EGG = the negated area (ideal instants, as D-026), so the method itself is tested, not EGG detection. The reference exactly as for PTDB-TUG (`closed_phase.analyse`, 10 kHz, order 12). 1,440 renders.
- *Measures*: per render, the median over 0.3–0.7 s of closed-phase F1–F3 minus the closed-glottis poles, and coverage (the share of frames the method returns; it drops frames whose closed phases are too short, a selection effect on real voices too). By condition: median over vowels with a vowel bootstrap.
- *Women's region*: women's size, f0 180 / 220 / 270, every open quotient and gap, render D (405 renders); pooled median F1 error with a vowel bootstrap (vowels resampled, all their renders kept).
- *Rule*: the current women's closed-phase F1 bias is about +8 Hz (teacher v3 +7.6; D-038's trunks +7.4). The reference **stands** for M3's F1-offset item if the women's-region median F1 error is within ±4 Hz (half that bias; D-026's point estimates were within 3 Hz). Otherwise **the reference's bias is material**: M3's F1-offset item then reports women's closed-phase bias both raw and corrected by this grid's bias at each frame's f0 and closed quotient (PTDB-TUG gives both per frame); the correction and the restated target are registered separately before use. A negative reference error means the models' overread is understated, a positive one overstated. *(Sign corrected 2026-10-28 22:40, after the result, before writing it up: backwards. The models' bias against the reference is (model − truth) − (reference − truth), so a reference reading **high** makes the models' overread look **smaller** than it is: positive = understated. The rule is symmetric (±4 Hz), so the decision is unaffected.)*
- *Reported*: the whole surface (F1–F3, coverage), the men's region (f0 100 / 140), the gap's and feedback's effects.
- *Seen before registering*: a two-render smoke run (women, f0 220 OQ 0.57 gap 0.05, vowel i: F1 +0.4 Hz; f0 330 OQ 0.70 gap 0.10, vowel I: F1 −7.8 Hz). Nothing else.
- *Limits*: one renderer (our physics; D-026 found it puts resonances on the labels), ideal instants, clean audio, static vowels. Real EGG detection errors and noise are a later extension if this finds a bias.
- **Result (2026-10-28 22:26; `reports/analysis/d043/cp_ref_grid.txt`, `d043_rule.txt`): the reference stands.** Women's region, F1 +2.9 [−0.1, +7.7] Hz (270 renders), within ±4. Every frame was returned (coverage 1.00 everywhere), so there is no selection effect at these settings.
  - *But the error is not flat; it follows the conditions of the overread*. Women's region F1 by closure gap: +1.2 [−1.3, +2.7] at 0, +3.2 [−1.0, +8.6] at 0.05, **+8.2 [+0.9, +12.5] at 0.10**; by open quotient: +1.1 at 0.45, +2.9 at 0.57, **+12.3 [−2.4, +16.8] at 0.70**. f0 matters little (+2.9 / +3.8 / +1.6 at 180 / 220 / 270). With a leaky closed phase, the "closed" phase still carries the subglottal coupling, so the reference reads the coupled F1, as the models do.
  - *So in breathy voices the models' true overread is likely larger than measured*, not smaller (the corrected sign above): where the reference reads +8 to +12 Hz high, an apparent +8 Hz model bias would be about +16 to +20 Hz against the closed-glottis resonance. How much this matters depends on how many real women's frames have such gaps, which the EGG cannot tell directly (it measures contact, not area).
  - F2 / F3: women's region +7.2 [+4.4, +9.3] / +7.7 [+6.7, +8.9] Hz; F3 rises steeply with the gap (+1.1 / +4.4 / **+19.9** at 0 / 0.05 / 0.10), as D-026's coupling check found for Praat. Men's region (f0 100 / 140): F1 +3.0 [+0.9, +4.6], F2 +6.1, F3 +7.1.
  - Feedback alone (gap 0): F1 +1.2 with it, −3.8 [−5.8, +2.6] without; D-026's two points (+3.0 / −0.1) are reproduced in kind.
  - *Next (roadmap item 8)*: (b) and (c) as planned. (c), the inverse-filtering test, is the one that can see past this: it asks which F1 leaves the flattest closed phase, which a leaky closure also disturbs, so its design must allow for a gap (registered separately).
- *Addendum, exploratory, reported only (Hannah, 2026-10-28 22:50, after the result above; registered before it runs)*: the same test up to 800 Hz, for singing (sopranos; VocalSet and MIR-1K reach there). Women's size, f0 330 (anchor) / 400 / 500 / 600 / 700 / 800 Hz, the same open quotients, gaps and vowels, render D; the cycle detection's f0 limit raised from 500 to 1,000 Hz (`analyse(..., f0_range=)`, new, default unchanged), since above 500 Hz it returns nothing. Two settings: order 12 (as used) and **order 8**. Mechanism expected from the code: covariance LPC predicts each closed-phase sample from the `order` samples before it (1.2 ms at order 12, 10 kHz), which reach back into the open phase once the closed phase is shorter than that: in part already at 330 Hz (closed phase 1–1.6 ms, a possible contributor to the open-quotient effect above), and for every sample at 800 Hz (period 1.25 ms). Order 8 reaches back 0.8 ms and still fits four poles below 5 kHz. Reported: F1–F3 error and coverage by f0, open quotient, gap and order; no decision. If order 8 is clearly better at 270–330 Hz, changing the reference for PTDB-TUG would be a separate registered entry (it would move every closed-phase number reported so far).
  - *Result (2026-10-28 22:55; `cp_ref_grid_high.txt`, `d043_high_summary.txt`; medians over vowels, open quotients and gaps, 90 renders per cell)*: **order 12 holds to about 400 Hz and fails from 500 Hz.** F1 signed / median |error|: 330 Hz +5.6 / 16.7; 400 Hz +4.8 / 25.5; 500 Hz +30.2 / 62.9; 600 Hz +14.0 / 65.2; 700 Hz +75.2 / 83.9; 800 Hz +113.8 / 210.2 (F2 +561 Hz there). Coverage stays 0.96–1.00, so the method returns numbers where they are wrong: above about 450 Hz a closed-phase F1 must not be used as a reference.
  - **Order 8 is worse everywhere** (F1 +32 at 330 Hz, about +500 Hz from 600 Hz; F2 / F3 off by tens to thousands of Hz). It underfits: at 10 kHz, four formants plus the source's and radiation's tilt need more than four pole pairs. So it cannot isolate the look-back mechanism, which stays untested; the one hint is at 330 Hz and open quotient 0.70, where order 8's F1 error (+14.7) is smaller than order 12's (+31.1), as a shorter look-back would predict. A cleaner test would keep order 12 and limit the predictors to closed-phase samples, which is impossible once the closed phase is shorter than the order. No change to the reference.
  - *Consequence*: PTDB-TUG's dev women (90th percentile 324 Hz) are inside the valid range. For sopranos there is no closed-phase reference; a reference there would need direct resonance measurement (broadband excitation at the lips during singing, as Joliveau, Smith & Wolfe 2004), not anything derived from the voice itself.

## D-044 (2026-10-28, pre-registered): label-free F1 checks by inverse filtering: a ripple test and a joint source-filter fit

Roadmap item 8 (c). Hannah: do both; space is cheap. Two criteria for "which F1 fits this voice", neither using a formant label. Both inverse-filter the voice with a candidate F1 and see how well what is left looks like a glottal source.

- *Why two, and what each can say (checked before registering, by tracing the criterion)*:
  - **R, the ripple test**: the leftover energy of the inverse-filtered signal in the closed phases. That is exactly what closed-phase covariance LPC (the reference) minimises over the same samples, so the reference's F1 is at or near R's optimum by construction. R therefore **cannot arbitrate** between the model and the reference. It is a robustness check of the reference: does its F1 survive when the rest of the filter is the model's own formants instead of LPC's free poles?
  - **J, the joint source-filter fit**: an LF pulse fitted to the inverse-filtered flow derivative over the **whole** cycle (analysis by synthesis, as ARX-LF methods in glottal inverse filtering). It uses the open phase and the source's shape, which the reference ignores, so the reference does not win by construction. J can arbitrate, but only if it passes the synthetic gate below.
  - *The caveat both share*: in a breathy voice "F1" is two things: the closed-glottis resonance (the training target, D-023's terms) and the effective resonance while the glottis is open, which coupling raises (D-043: the reference drifts towards it with a leaky closure). Stage 1 shows which one each criterion sees.
- *Common method* (`scripts/analysis/d044_inverse.py`, CPU; teacher v3 inference on GPU or CPU): 10 kHz and pre-emphasis as the reference; per frame the cycles wholly within ±20 ms, instants from the EGG (`egg_instants`, `speech_lag`) on real voices and from the area on synthetic ones. Inverse filter: an all-pole filter with the candidate F1 and B1, and fixed F2–F4 with their bandwidths. Candidates: F1 on a grid of ±300 cents around the reference's F1 in 10-cent steps; B1 at 40 / 60 / 90 / 130 / 200 / 300 Hz; the optimum over both is the criterion's F1.
  - R: the closed phases' (GCI + 0.3 ms to GOI − 0.3 ms) share of the whole cycles' energy of the filtered signal, summed over the frame's cycles.
  - J: per cycle, an LF flow derivative (`synth.glottal.lf_waveform`) with Rd on a grid of 25 values from 0.3 to 2.7, aligned so its main excitation falls at the GCI (±0.3 ms in 0.1 ms steps), amplitude by least squares; the residual energy over the whole cycle relative to the cycle's energy, minimised per cycle and summed over the frame.
- **Stage 1, synthetic gate**, on D-043's renders (render D, both sizes, f0 100–330 Hz, every open quotient and gap; F2–F4 and bandwidths at their true values; also with F2–F4 moved by ±3 % as a model's errors would, reported):
  - R passes if the women's region (D-043's: women's size, f0 180 / 220 / 270, all open quotients and gaps) median F1 error is within ±4 Hz.
  - J passes **as an arbiter** if the women's-region median F1 error is within ±4 Hz **and**, in the leaky cells (gap 0.10, and open quotient 0.70 separately), its |median error| is at least 4 Hz smaller than the reference's (D-043: +8.2 and +12.3 Hz). If within ±4 Hz overall but not better in the leaky cells, J is **unbiased but not independent of the reference where it matters**: stage 2 runs, reported only. If outside ±4 Hz overall, J stops and is reported.
  - Reported: both criteria's error surfaces, beside D-043's reference surface; the correlation over cells of J's error with the reference's error (near 1 would mean J sees the same thing).
- **Stage 2, real voices** (only what stage 1 allows): PTDB-TUG **dev** speakers (6 women, 6 men), the frames of `cache/cp/ptdb_egg_sub.npz` with a finite reference F1 and teacher v3's F1. F2–F4 and bandwidths from teacher v3 (rerun on those utterances for F4 and the bandwidths). Per frame, signed differences: teacher v3 − F1_J, reference − F1_J, teacher v3 − F1_R, reference − F1_R. Medians by sex, speaker bootstrap (2,000).
  - *Rule (J, if it passed as an arbiter)*: **the model's overread is confirmed** if women's median (teacher v3 − F1_J) ≥ +5 Hz with its interval excluding 0; **the reference reads high** if women's median (reference − F1_J) ≥ +5 Hz with its interval excluding 0. Both can hold. If teacher v3 − F1_J is within ±5 Hz and its interval includes 0, **the overread is not confirmed** by the joint fit.
  - *Consequence for item 8*: overread confirmed → the domain discriminator next, to find what the synthesiser lacks; the reference reads high and the overread is not confirmed → item 8's F1 offset is reported against F1_J as well as the reference before any fix is registered.
  - *Noise floor, reported with stage 2*: each frame's criterion F1 from odd and even cycles separately; the SD of their difference.
- *Seen before registering*: nothing of either criterion; D-043's results (reference surface) are known.
- *Limits*: one source model (LF) in J; the Rosenberg-pulse renders are not LF, so J's synthetic residual is never zero (a realistic handicap: real sources are not LF either). Instants from the EGG on real voices carry detection error, which the synthetic gate does not have. Dev speakers only; no semi-private or lockbox use.
- *Amendment before stage 1 (2026-10-28 23:40; seen: two gate renders in a smoke run, then development renders off the gate's grid only: women, f0 160 / 240, open quotient 0.50 / 0.65, gaps 0 / 0.03 / 0.08, every third vowel)*:
  1. **Absolute criteria.** As registered, R and J were ratios to the cycles' energy. With a monic inverse filter, a ratio is minimised by inflating the denominator (the smoke run: R −134 Hz on a render where the reference was −3). Both are now absolute least-squares energies: the reference's own criterion for R, ARX-LF's for J.
  2. **A nuisance filter.** With only F1–F4 in the inverse filter, F1 moved to stand in for the source's tilt and the resonances above F4, which the reference's order 12 has room for (R +16 to +36 Hz against the reference's ±4). Each candidate now gets a free monic order-4 remainder, fitted by covariance least squares on the closed phases, separately for the pre-emphasised (R) and raw (J) signals. With it, R matches the reference on the development renders (+3.7 against +3.8 Hz median).
  3. *Seen on the development renders, not changed*: J reads F1 high by about +30 Hz with feedback, and **+63 Hz without feedback or gap** (render C, a pure source-filter render), where R reads +0.0. The renders' source is a Rosenberg pulse, so J's LF templates pull F1 to make the flow look LF-shaped: a source-model mismatch, not coupling. Real sources are not LF either. Adding Rosenberg templates would fit the test rather than the voice, so J runs as it is, and the gate decides.
- **Stage 1 result (2026-10-28 23:05; `reports/analysis/d044/d044_stage1.txt`; 3,240 renders): R passes; J fails and stops.**
  - *Women's region* (F1 error against the closed-glottis pole, Hz): reference +2.4 [+0.3, +5.5], **R +1.3 [−1.1, +2.3]**, **J +30.1 [+19.0, +36.6]**. Men's region: reference +3.0, R +2.3, J +27.0.
  - *J*: the source-model bias seen on the development renders, everywhere (correlation of its cell errors with the reference's +0.19: it is wrong in its own way). With an LF source model it cannot arbitrate. Stops here, as registered.
  - *R, unexpectedly, sees past the leaky closure*: gap 0.10: reference +8.8, R **+2.0**; open quotient 0.70: reference +12.1, R **+1.6**. Holding F2–F4 at their true values (and the order-4 remainder) leaves F1 less room to follow the coupled resonance than order-12 LPC has. Its cell errors still correlate +0.81 with the reference's, so it is a constrained relative, as expected.
  - *But R depends on F2–F4*: moved by +3 % / −3 %, R's women's-region F1 error becomes +10.4 [+5.6, +15.1] / +5.3 [+2.7, +8.4]. On real voices F2–F4 come from teacher v3, whose errors there are a few per cent, so R's real-voice F1 carries about 5–10 Hz of that model's error. As registered, R's stage 2 is reported only.
  - *Stage 2*: R on PTDB-TUG dev, reported. J does not run.
- **Stage 2 result, R, reported only (2026-10-29 00:41; `reports/analysis/d044/d044_stage2.txt`; PTDB-TUG dev, 3,867 women's and 3,876 men's frames, 6 speakers each; teacher v3's rerun matched the cached F1–F3 to 0.00 Hz)**: median F1 differences, speaker bootstrap. Women: teacher v3 − reference +6.2 [+2.5, +10.8]; **teacher v3 − R +1.9 [−2.3, +6.4]**; reference − R −2.4 [−3.2, 0.0]. Men: teacher v3 − reference +6.6 [+3.2, +10.0]; teacher v3 − R +5.2 [+1.6, +9.0]; reference − R 0.0 (R's grid often picks its centre, the reference's F1; the step is about 3 Hz). R's own noise (odd against even cycles): women SD 49 Hz (median |.| 11.8), men 18.5 (6.5). *Reading, no rule*: against R, women's overread shrinks to an interval including 0 while men's stays, but R carries teacher v3's F2–F4 errors (5–10 Hz of F1 per 3 % in stage 1) and is noisy for women, so this cannot separate the model's error from R's. It does not change item 8's order.
  - *Why R beats the reference although it minimises the same quantity (checked after stage 1, Hannah's question; `scripts/analysis/d044_nuisance_roots.py`, women's region, frame at 0.5 s)*. Suspected: the order-4 remainder places a pole near F1 and absorbs the coupled shift. **Refuted**: the remainder has a pole within 0.6–1.6 × F1 in 0 % of renders at gap 0, 6 % at 0.05 and 11 % at 0.10 / open quotient 0.70; those poles are about 3.6 kHz wide (tilt, not a resonance), and the remainder's poles otherwise sit at 1.4–4.6 kHz (F5 region and tilt). R's advantage holds where the remainder has no pole near F1 (gap 0.10: R +1.9, reference +8.8; open quotient 0.70: +0.7 against +8.5). So it is the constraint: with F2–F4 pinned and F1 one resonance, R cannot spend spare poles on what the coupling adds to a leaky "closed" phase (subglottal poles and zeros), which is what moves order-12 LPC's F1. The same constraint is why R moves with F2–F4's errors.

## D-045 (2026-10-28, pre-registered): a domain discriminator: where do real voices differ from the synthetic ones, and does it line up with women's F1 overread?

Roadmap item 8 (b), after D-043 (the reference stands, but drifts with leaky closure) and D-044 (J cannot arbitrate). Hannah: the most informative remaining check, and much cheaper than training runs. A classifier telling synthetic from real frames shows what the synthesiser lacks. Applied to PTDB-TUG dev frames, it shows whether the frames that look least synthetic are the overread ones. It separates synthetic from real, never one speaker from another.

- *Data, never PTDB-TUG for training* (an evaluation set): real voices from two training-pool corpora, so a finding specific to one corpus (language, microphone) does not count unless the other agrees.
  - **Zeroth-Korean, test split** (FLAC, adult read speech; numeric ids and recorded sex only, never names). Not the train split, whose resynthesised versions teacher v3 trained on.
  - **speechocean762, adults** (age ≥ 18 by its `spk2age`; WAV; L2 English read speech; sex from `spk2gender`).
  - Ruled out: LibriTTS-R (its restoration passes every file through a neural vocoder, rewriting the source detail sought here), MLS (Opus), Common Voice (MP3, noisy).
  - **Synthetic**: train_v2 clips (tube and cascade), with teacher v3's channel augmentation, as training presents them.
  - *Frames*: 10 ms frames that teacher v3 calls voiced and sonorant (both probabilities > 0.5). Up to 20,000 frames per class and sex, drawn evenly over at least 40 speakers (real; or all a split has) or 400 clips (synthetic).
- *Discriminators*, per real corpus and per sex (four of each kind). Synthetic frames are reweighted to the real sex's f0 distribution (importance weights from 1/6-octave f0 histograms), so f0 alone cannot separate them. L2-regularised logistic regression on standardised inputs; strength by inner cross-validation; 5-fold cross-validation grouped by speaker (real) and clip (synthetic) for the reported AUC; then refitted on all.
  - **D_h**: teacher v3's encoder output h (what the formant heads read). Answers *whether* the gap lines up with the overread.
  - **D_a**: interpretable measures per frame (40 ms windows): f0; H1–H2 and H1–A1 (dB; A1 at the harmonic nearest teacher v3's F1); cepstral peak prominence; harmonics-to-noise ratio (Praat, via parselmouth); spectral tilt (dB per octave, 100 Hz–4 kHz); energy above 4 kHz relative to 0–4 kHz; local jitter and shimmer (Praat, ±50 ms); teacher v3's B1–B3. Answers *what* the synthesiser lacks. Measures that use teacher v3's formants carry its errors; noted, not corrected.
- *Applied to* PTDB-TUG **dev** frames (`cache/cp/ptdb_egg_sub.npz`: 6 women, 6 men; teacher v3's F1 and the closed-phase reference per frame; scoring only).
- **Rule 1, D_h locates the overread**: for women, Spearman(logit of D_h's real-ness, teacher v3's F1 − closed-phase F1) ≥ **0.2**, with its speaker-bootstrap interval (2,000) excluding 0, **for both corpora's D_h**. Null: 20 discriminators trained with the class labels shuffled within sex; their Spearman values are reported, and the observed value must lie above all 20. Men are reported as the contrast.
- **Rule 2, what the synthesiser lacks** (only if rule 1 holds): a D_a measure is a **candidate** if, for women, its f0-matched synthetic-against-real AUC differs from 0.5 by ≥ 0.2 in both corpora, **and** on PTDB-TUG dev women its frame values correlate with teacher v3's F1 error (|Spearman| ≥ 0.15, speaker-bootstrap interval excluding 0). Candidates name the synthesiser change, registered as its own entry. If rule 1 fails in both corpora: the gap the heads can see does not line up with the overread; reported, and D_a is still reported in full.
- *Reported*: cross-validated AUCs; D_a's per-measure AUCs and standardised coefficients by corpus and sex; D_h's real-ness against closed quotient and f0 on PTDB-TUG dev; the overlap of the two corpora's D_h directions (cosine of the coefficient vectors).
- *Noise and power checked before registering*: 6 women's speakers give a coarse speaker bootstrap. The 0.2 threshold and the requirement of both corpora are set against that; a pass needs a clear effect.
- *Code*: `scripts/analysis/d045_discriminator.py` (features: GPU in short bursts beside D-040; measures and fits: CPU). Nothing seen: no features extracted yet.
- *Amendment before anything ran (Hannah, 2026-10-28 23:55; nothing seen)*: **children and age, reported only.** (1) Children: the same discriminators for speechocean762's children (age < 18) and Samrómur's children (`C:\github\datasets\samromur_children`, after its licence and metadata are checked and recorded in `ATTRIBUTION.md`; if it cannot be used, speechocean762 alone and the result is marked single-corpus), applied to Hillenbrand **dev** boys and girls against Hillenbrand's labels (LPC with hand checking, which reads F1 low: never a rule here). (2) Age: D_h's real-ness against age within speechocean762's adults (Zeroth-Korean has no ages). Neither changes rules 1 and 2.
- *Amendment, reported only (Hannah, 2026-10-29 00:50; nothing of D-045 run or seen; D-046's stage 1 known)*: QCP-FB as context, at D-046's arm B settings fixed now (duration quotient 0.3, 10 kHz, SEDREAMS GCIs; not adopted as a reference, having narrowly failed D-046's gate). (1) On PTDB-TUG dev, rule 1's Spearman is also reported with teacher v3 − QCP-FB F1 in place of teacher v3 − closed-phase F1. (2) In the children's arm, Hillenbrand dev children's F1 error is reported against QCP-FB as well as against Hillenbrand's labels. Neither enters rule 1 or 2.
- *Code and smoke run (2026-10-29 01:10; `scripts/analysis/d045_discriminator.py`)*: features on 16 synthetic clips, all 10 Zeroth test speakers, 6 speechocean762 speakers and 4 PTDB-TUG utterances (one speaker), to check the code path. *Seen*: the per-source medians of the measures (e.g. H1–H2 about 7 dB on synthetic frames against 1–3 dB on the real ones, on that handful); no discriminator output was read (the fit was run to check it completes). Zeroth's test split has 10 speakers (6 women, 4 men), fewer than the 40 aimed for; as registered, all of them are used. H1–A1 is 0 where the harmonic nearest F1 is H1 itself (high f0 or low F1); kept as defined.
- *Limits*: frame-level, linear. A non-linear gap would be missed (a later extension if rule 1 fails). Zeroth is Korean and speechocean762 is L2 English, while PTDB-TUG is native English and German read speech; the f0 and sex matching does not remove language.
- **Result (2026-10-29 01:33; `reports/analysis/d045/d045.txt`; synthetic 44,820 frames from 800 clips; Zeroth test 9,960 women's / 8,320 men's frames from 6 / 4 speakers; speechocean762 adults 19,944 / 19,992 from 72 / 56; PTDB-TUG dev 7,743 frames): rule 1 fails, so rule 2 is not evaluated.**
  - *D_h separates perfectly but does not locate the overread*: cross-validated AUC 1.000 / 1.000 (Zeroth women / men) and 0.999 / 0.999 (speechocean762); on PTDB-TUG dev women, Spearman(real-ness, teacher v3 − closed-phase F1) −0.012 [−0.072, +0.036] (Zeroth) and +0.008 [−0.063, +0.068] (speechocean762), inside their label-shuffled nulls (max +0.095 / +0.074). Men −0.034 / −0.026. With QCP-FB's F1 instead (reported): +0.004 / +0.036. The two corpora's D_h directions largely agree (cosine 0.81 women, 0.71 men).
  - *Caveat*: with perfect separation, the score's variation inside the real class can follow something incidental (recording channel) rather than graded "unlike synthetic"; this is a negative for a linear, frame-level gap, as the limits said, not for every gap.
  - *Reported*: real-ness on PTDB-TUG rises with f0 (women +0.26 [+0.09, +0.39] Zeroth; +0.14 speechocean762) and falls with closed quotient (−0.11 / −0.10; men −0.27 / −0.14): breathier, higher voices look less like the synthetic data. D_a: AUC 0.85 / 0.65 (Zeroth women / men), 0.86 / 0.80 (speechocean762). The strongest single measures are teacher v3's B2 and B3 (real voices wider; AUC 0.65–0.83), then tilt, H1–H2, CPP (about 0.36–0.66). PTDB women: HNR and B2 track the F1 error weakly (+0.11 each), H1–H2 −0.10. Real-ness against age, speechocean762 adults: women −0.28 (72 speakers), men +0.08.
  - *Reading for roadmap item 8*: D-043 (the reference stands, drifts with leaky closure), D-044 (a constrained fit sees past the drift but carries teacher v3's F2–F4), D-046 (QCP-FB nearly good enough, not adopted) and now D-045 (no linear frame-level link between the synthetic/real gap and the overread): the F1 offset is real against every reference here, and the cheap probes have not located its cause. Still to do: the children's arm (Samrómur licence check; Hillenbrand dev children).
- *Exploratory addendum, reported only (Hannah, 2026-10-29; set before running; seen: the three weak correlations above, against the closed-phase reference only)*: **do the H1–H2, HNR and B2 correlations come from the reference?** D-043 found the closed-phase reference reads F1 high with leaky closure and a large open quotient, which would shrink teacher v3 − reference in breathier voices and produce exactly these signs even with a constant teacher error. On the same PTDB-TUG dev frames, all 12 D_a inputs are correlated (Spearman, speaker bootstrap 2,000) with teacher v3's F1 error against three references: closed-phase, D-044's R (robust to leaky closure in D-044 stage 1) and QCP-FB (D-046 arm B, 10 kHz, DQ 0.3), and with the references' own differences (closed-phase − R, closed-phase − QCP-FB). Added: the mean within-speaker Spearman (removing between-speaker differences; 6 speakers per sex), and a noise floor from 200 within-speaker shuffles of each measure (95th percentile of |ρ|). *Reading, fixed now*: a correlation that holds against R and QCP-FB (same sign, interval excluding 0) belongs to teacher v3; one that vanishes there and appears in closed-phase − R belongs to the reference. B2 is teacher v3's own output, so its link to teacher v3's error is weak evidence either way. Women and men. Code: `scripts/analysis/d045_confound.py`.
  - *Result (2026-10-29; `reports/analysis/d045/d045_confound.txt`)*: **the confound guess is wrong; HNR belongs to teacher v3.** Closed-phase − R does not track H1–H2 (+0.005) or HNR (+0.003) in women, so the reference's leaky-closure drift does not show along these measures on real voices.
    - *HNR*: holds against all three references in both sexes (women +0.11 / +0.12 / +0.11 against closed-phase / R / QCP-FB; men +0.10 / +0.10 / +0.10), within speaker too, above the shuffle null, and absent from the reference differences. Size (added after the table was seen; medians by within-speaker HNR quartile, least to most harmonic): women's error against closed-phase −0.1, +3.4, +7.6, +14.7 Hz; against R −4.9, −0.9, +2.7, +11.2; men +3.1 → +10.0. The overread sits mostly in the most harmonic frames. Real voices are more harmonic than the synthetic ones (D_a's HNR AUC 0.60–0.61 for women), so this is a lead for a domain-gap cause (renders noisier than real voices), not a finding: HNR also follows vowel nucleus, level and position.
    - *H1–H2*: −0.10 against closed-phase and R, but +0.08 (interval spans 0) against QCP-FB, and closed-phase − QCP-FB tracks it (+0.10): QCP-FB has its own H1–H2 dependence. Women only. Unresolved.
    - *B2*: +0.11 / +0.18 / +0.10 (women; QCP-FB's interval spans 0), men +0.11 / +0.10 / +0.07. R's F1 itself tracks B2, HF energy, tilt and H1–A1 in women (closed-phase − R +0.20, +0.22, +0.16, +0.15): R carries teacher v3's F2–F4, so its F1 depends on spectral shape. B2 stays weak evidence.
- *Follow-ups registered 2026-10-29 (Hannah; reported only, no training decision; seen beforehand: D-045's results, its addendum and D-047; for the band check, only the measure on D-047's renders below)*. Code: `scripts/analysis/d045_probes.py`.
  - **A. Band periodic fraction, renders against real voices.** SoFilNet's aperiodicity target (Meka et al. 2026, eqs 11–12): per 50 ms Hann frame, the lag of the strongest autocorrelation peak of the 0–1.5 kHz component within 60–400 Hz, then per band (0–1, 1–2, 2–4, 4–6, 6–8 kHz) the band's autocorrelation at that lag over its value at 0, corrected for the window's own autocorrelation. Frames: Praat-voiced (cc, 60–400 Hz), the same rule for every source, up to 40 per file. Sources: 400 fresh train_v2 renders (seed 4548), clean and channelled as teacher v3 trained (whole-clip whispers dropped); Zeroth-Korean test and speechocean762 adults, up to 12,000 frames per corpus and sex (training pools only: this could shape synthesis priors, so never an evaluation set). Synthetic frames reweighted to each real sex's f0 distribution (1/6-octave bins). *Reading*, per sex and band, clean renders: the band **differs** if real − synthetic has a speaker / clip bootstrap interval excluding 0 with the same sign in both corpora, and its size in both exceeds the Zeroth − speechocean762 difference (the between-corpus scale). No band differs → "profiles agree"; otherwise the bands are named. Channelled renders reported beside. *Checked before registering*: on D-047's paired women's renders (clips 0–19), the measure is higher at 40 than at 5 dB in every band (19, 20, 20, 19 and 14 of 20 clips from the lowest band up); even at 40 dB the 2–4 kHz band reads only 0.45, from jitter, shimmer and vibrato inside 50 ms.
  - **B. Discriminator probes** (D_h refitted exactly as registered, on D-045's cached frames; new frames scored with it).
    1. *Occlusion*: teacher v3 features of fresh channelled renders (400 clips, up to 30 frames each) and real voices (Zeroth test, speechocean762 adults; about 4,000 frames per corpus and sex) under four conditions, the frames chosen once on the unaltered audio: full; low-pass 4 kHz; low-pass 2 kHz; white noise 30 dB below the file's mean power (masks low-level differences such as the background). D_h refitted per condition with D-045's cross-validation. *Reading*: an occlusion "removes the separation" if the women's cross-validated AUC falls below 0.95 in both corpora; otherwise "no occlusion removes it".
    2. *Generator settings*: each fresh render's known settings (`hnr_db`, jitter, shimmer, tube or cascade, tract length, nasal / subglottal extras, median Rd and f0, the channel's scalar draws) against its mean real-ness under each corpus's women's D_h; Spearman with a clip bootstrap (1,000), ranked. Reported only.
    3. *Resynthesised training clips* (Zeroth-Korean and VocalSet train, 200 clips each, channelled as trained): their real-ness against real and synthetic frames; the share scored real. Reported only: near real → the cue is in the source and rendering details the resynthesis keeps from our synthesiser; near synthetic → in the dynamics the resynthesis takes from real audio.
    4. *D-047's paired renders* (60 women's-range clips, 5 and 40 dB, channelled): the paired change in real-ness, beside the real-against-synthetic median gap for scale. Reported only.
    - *Not done here*: gradient maps over the VQT input (`considering.md`, Synthesiser), until the occlusion says where to look.
  - *Result A (2026-10-29 12:48; `reports/analysis/d045/bpf.txt`): **renders differ in 0–1 and 1–2 kHz, for women and men**; real voices are less periodic there.* Clean renders against real (Zeroth / speechocean762): women 0–1 kHz −0.015 / −0.025, **1–2 kHz −0.159 / −0.150** (renders 0.68–0.69 against real 0.53); men 0–1 kHz −0.07 / −0.07, 1–2 kHz −0.22 / −0.17. From 2 kHz up the profiles agree within the corpora's own spread (|differences| ≤ 0.06, intervals mostly spanning 0). Channelled renders narrow the gap but keep 1–2 kHz (women −0.07, men −0.13 / −0.08), and leave men's 0–1 kHz (−0.03).
    - So our aperiodicity rises too steeply with frequency: too little aperiodic energy below 2 kHz relative to the harmonics, about right above. Consistent with the aspiration's fixed shape (differentiated white noise, +6 dB/octave, before the tract). Other candidates for real voices' low-band aperiodicity, not separated here: faster f0 and amplitude movement inside 50 ms than our contours have, background and room, a DC leak.
    - *Against D-047*: its 5 dB arm reached real voices' 1–2 kHz value (0.40 against 0.53) without moving the F1 bias, but only by making the bands above 2 kHz far too aperiodic; the right *shape* was never tested. A noise-shape arm (`considering.md`, Synthesiser: noise model upgrades) is now the concrete candidate.
  - *Result B (2026-10-29 12:55; `reports/analysis/d045/probes.txt`).* D_h refits as registered (CV AUC 1.000 / 1.000 / 0.999 / 0.999).
    1. **No occlusion removes the separation**: women's CV AUC under full / low-pass 4 kHz / low-pass 2 kHz / 30 dB noise floor: Zeroth 0.998 / 0.999 / 1.000 / 0.999, speechocean762 0.996 / 0.994 / 0.997 / 0.997 (men likewise). The cue is not the recording chain's high band or background, and it survives below 2 kHz.
    2. *Settings that make renders look real* (clip-mean real-ness, Zeroth / speechocean762 women's D_h; renders' median −20 / −14 against real frames' +10 / +8): higher f0 (+0.50 / +0.44); a codec, and a lower bitrate (−0.35 / −0.37 with kbps); more reverberation (direct-to-reverberant ratio −0.31 / −0.27); any channel (clean −0.19 / −0.21); the cascade over the tube (−0.18 / −0.28); cleaner aspiration (`hnr_db` +0.15 / +0.14). Jitter, shimmer, Rd, SNR, filters and the nasal / subglottal extras: none clear. Whatever smears the renders' fine harmonic detail in time or frequency (codec, reverberation) makes them look more real, while more of our aspiration makes them look *less* real: consistent with result A, where our noise is about right above 2 kHz and missing below it.
    3. *Resynthesised training clips sit between*: median real-ness −1.2 (VocalSet) and −4.2 (Zeroth) against real +10.3 and renders −20.2 (Zeroth women's D_h; the other three alike); 27–44 % (VocalSet) and 14–28 % (Zeroth) scored real. Real dynamics (f0, level and formant contours from real audio) close most of the gap; the rest lies in what resynthesis keeps from our synthesiser (source, noise, rendering).
    4. *D-047's arms*: 40 − 5 dB moves real-ness +1.0 [+0.5, +1.6] / +2.0 [+1.3, +2.7] logits, against a real − synthetic gap of +31 / +22: aspiration level is a minor cue.
    - *Reading for the synthesiser* (`considering.md`, Synthesiser): two candidates now have evidence: realistic contours (3) and noise whose share falls less steeply with frequency, more aperiodic energy below 2 kHz (A, 2). Neither has been shown to touch the F1 bias; D-045's rule 1 found no frame-level link between real-ness and the overread.


## D-046 (2026-10-29, pre-registered): QCP-FB as a formant reference for voices without an EGG

Hannah: run it alongside D-045, so D-045's children's arm can use a better reference than Hillenbrand's LPC labels. Literature: `docs/papers/literature.md` (Airaksinen et al. 2014; Gowda, Airaksinen & Alku 2017, read in full; the authors' weight code, Apache-2.0, read as a specification).

- *Why*: our only resonance-oriented reference, closed-phase LPC (D-019, D-043), needs an EGG; only PTDB-TUG and ARCTIC have one. Hillenbrand (children included) has LPC labels with hand checking, which read F1 low (D-019's follow-up). QCP-FB estimates the closures from the speech, weights the prediction error towards the closed phase (no hard selection), and predicts each sample from both sides (more equations per short closed phase).
- *Implementation* (`vocal_analysis/eval/qcp.py`, tested; `scripts/analysis/d046_qcp.py`): as published in Gowda et al. (2017):
  - weighted forward-backward covariance LP (their eqs. 14–18);
  - QCP weights as the authors' `qcp_wt`: per cycle, 1 from GCI + 0.05 T0 for 0.7 T0, linear ramps of 0.875 ms (7 samples at 8 kHz) at each end, 10⁻⁵ elsewhere;
  - the sample rate is a parameter, default 8 kHz (as published); settings kept in time units so they scale: order 13 at 8 kHz, scaled with the rate (15 at 10 kHz, 21 at 16 kHz), 30 ms rectangular frames, pre-emphasis 1 − 0.97 z⁻¹;
  - GCIs from SEDREAMS (Drugman et al. 2012), reimplemented from the paper;
  - formants from the predictor's roots with `closed_phase.lpc_formants`' rules, as our reference, so the two differ only in the fit. The paper's Gaussian-derivative peak picking is reported as a variant.
  - Also reported: forward-only QCP, and QCP-FB with ideal GCIs (from the glottal area on synthetic audio), which separates GCI error from the method's.
- *Rates (Hannah)*: stage 1 runs at **8 kHz** (as published), **10 kHz** (our closed-phase reference's) and **16 kHz**. 8 kHz is the candidate; a higher rate replaces it if it passes every stage-1 criterion and its women's-region median |F1 error| is not larger (F3 has more headroom there). The rate is chosen on synthetic data only, before stage 2 runs.
- **Two arms (amendment before registering, from development renders off the grid; Hannah: go ahead)**. On the development renders (women, f0 160 / 240, open quotient 0.50 / 0.65, ideal lag-shifted closures; `scripts/analysis/d046_dq_diag.py`), the published weight (1 for **0.7** of the period from GCI + 0.05 T0) read F1 **+21 Hz** with feedback (render D) and **−45 Hz** without (render C); a weight kept inside the closed phase (0.3) read **−1.3** and −13.6 Hz. The 0.7 weight takes in the start of the next open phase, which drags F1 down through the source's open-phase energy and up through the coupled open-glottis resonance. Its parameters were tuned on LF-source vowels, whose return phase stretches the effective closed phase.
  - **Arm A, as published (duration quotient 0.7)**: the faithful baseline, reported (expected to fail).
  - **Arm B, duration quotient 0.3**: the **candidate for adoption**. The value comes from **PTDB-TUG dev women's EGG**, not the renders: their 10th-percentile closed quotient is 0.31, so a 0.3 weight stays inside the closed phase for about 90 % of real women's frames. The renders showed only that the weight must stay inside the closed phase.
  - Every stage-1 and stage-2 criterion below applies to arm B; the rate choice (8 / 10 / 16 kHz) is made for arm B. Arm A runs at 8 kHz only. Ideal closures are the area's, shifted by the glottis-to-lips lag `speech_lag` measures (SEDREAMS' identification rate is scored against the same).
  - *Later, its own entry*: a weight ending at an opening instant detected from speech (no fixed quotient; opening detection from speech is much less reliable).
- **Stage 1, synthetic** (D-043's grid, render D, both sizes, f0 100–330 Hz, and the addendum's 400–800 Hz grid; F1–F3 against the closed-glottis poles; the closed-phase reference beside it, as D-043 measured it). Also at 30 dB SNR (white noise), reported.
  - *GCIs first*: SEDREAMS must find at least 95 % of the true closures within ±0.5 ms on the 100–330 Hz grid (identification rate, by f0); otherwise stop and report.
  - *Paired with the reference*: the closed-phase reference is recomputed on exactly the same renders and frame times (10 kHz, order 12, ideal instants, as D-043), so every comparison is paired and its tolerances come from the reference's own uncertainty, not from chosen numbers (Hannah). "Half-width" below is half the width of the reference's vowel-bootstrap 95 % interval (2,000) for that statistic, in that region.
  - *Pass* (QCP-FB with SEDREAMS GCIs), all of:
    1. *Bias*: women's-region (D-043's) and men's-region (f0 100 / 140) F1 median error within ±4 Hz. Kept as a number on purpose: it is derived from the use (half the women's F1 bias the reference exists to measure), not from noise.
    2. *Leaky closure*: at gap 0.10 and at open quotient 0.70 (women's region), |QCP-FB's median F1 error| ≤ |the reference's| + the reference's half-width there.
    3. *F2 and F3*: women's-region |median error| ≤ |the reference's| + its half-width (on D-043's numbers about 9.7 and 8.8 Hz).
    4. *Precision*: the paired per-render difference |QCP-FB F1 error| − |reference F1 error|, women's region; the upper bound of its vowel-bootstrap 95 % interval ≤ the reference's half-width for median |F1 error|.
    5. *Coverage*: frames returned ≥ 90 %. A usability floor, not a statistical claim; the reference returns 100 % with ideal instants, which a method that must find its own closures cannot be held to.
  - *Valid f0 range*: the f0 cells (women's size) whose median |F1 error| over open quotients and gaps is within 8 Hz; QCP-FB references are used only inside it.
- **Stage 2, PTDB-TUG dev** (only if stage 1 passes; both references exist there): per frame, QCP-FB (no EGG) against the closed-phase reference (EGG), F1, by sex. No fixed tolerance (Hannah: avoid a magic number); the agreement is **predicted from stage 1** instead:
