  - *Prediction*: stage 1's median of (QCP-FB − closed-phase) F1 over the same renders, in the women's and men's regions, with its vowel-bootstrap interval. It says how far the two references should disagree if real voices behave like the renders.
  - *Agrees* if, for both sexes, the speaker-bootstrap (2,000) 95 % interval of the real-voice median difference overlaps the prediction's interval. Then each reference's synthetic bias carries over, and QCP-FB is adopted.
  - *Precision*: an interval can overlap simply by being wide (6 speakers per sex). So the real-voice interval's half-width must be at most **4 Hz**, the same reasoning as D-043's bound: a reference must resolve half the women's F1 bias it exists to measure (about +8 Hz). If it is wider, the result is **inconclusive**, not agreement, and QCP-FB is not adopted on this evidence.
  - *Disagrees* (precise, no overlap): real voices hold something the renders lack (GCI errors, breathiness); QCP-FB is not adopted, and the difference is investigated.
  - F2 / F3 differences and the frame-level spread are reported.
  - *Amendment, reported only (Hannah's question, 2026-10-29 00:35; stage 1 running, nothing seen)*: arm A also runs on PTDB-TUG dev. Its real-voice difference from arm B, (A − B) F1 by sex with a speaker bootstrap, is set beside stage 1's synthetic (A − B). A real gap clearly smaller than the synthetic one would say our renderer's subglottal coupling is too strong (D-026's coupling check already found Praat's F3 rising with the opening more than Birkholz et al. did); a similar gap, that it is realistic. Reported, no rule; a finding there would be a synthesiser entry of its own.
- **Adoption**: if both stages pass, QCP-FB becomes the formant reference for voices without an EGG, inside its valid f0 range: Hillenbrand dev (all four groups) and D-045's children's arm, reported **beside** the existing labels (Hillenbrand's own labels stay; every comparison is shown against both). On EGG sets the closed-phase reference stays primary and QCP-FB is reported beside it. Adoption changes no earlier decision; using the new reference for a decision needs that decision's own entry.
- *Before registering, done*: `vocal_analysis/eval/qcp.py`, 9 tests (`tests/test_qcp.py`: the weights match `qcp_wt`'s construction on a worked example; known poles recovered from free decay; the QCP-weighted fit returns exactly four formants despite open-phase noise; SEDREAMS finds ≥ 95 % of closures at f0 100 / 200 / 300 Hz; the end-to-end analysis reads the poles with ideal GCIs); commit `31e0387`. Smoke runs on development renders only (above). SEDREAMS is implemented from the published method; GLOAT's code (GPL-3.0) was consulted, not copied. The gate grid has not been run.
- *Limits*: one physical renderer; SEDREAMS assumes a reasonably clear excitation, so breathy or noisy voices may lose GCIs (the coverage and the 30 dB check report it); 8 kHz analysis limits F3 to well below 4 kHz (the paper's choice, kept).
- **Stage 1 result (2026-10-29 00:29; `reports/analysis/d046/d046_gate.txt`, `d046_stage1.txt`, `d046_stage1_high.txt`; 1,080 renders, plus 540 at 400–800 Hz): no arm passes, so QCP-FB is not adopted and stage 2 does not run** (nor arm A's coupling check, which was part of it).
  - *GCIs*: SEDREAMS found 99.4 % of the lag-shifted closures (97.0 % at 330 Hz); the gate passes.
  - *Arm A (as published)*: women's-region F1 **+16.6** Hz, men's +10.3; leaky cells +20.3 / +29.0; fails every criterion but coverage. As expected from the development renders.
  - *Arm B*: unbiased on F1 at every rate (women's region +2.1 / +3.6 / +3.1 Hz at 8 / 10 / 16 kHz; men's +0.1 / −0.0 / +0.8), and better than the reference with a leaky closure (gap 0.10: +4.5 / +6.4 / +6.6 against +8.8). Each rate misses one or two paired criteria, narrowly: 8 kHz F3 −17.1 (bound 8.6) and precision (+4.7 against 3.3); **10 kHz precision only** (paired upper bound +4.0 against the reference's half-width 3.1); **16 kHz F3 only** (+10.1 against 8.6). The reference is very consistent over vowels on F3 (half-width 1.1 Hz), which makes that bound tight.
  - *Forward-only QCP (duration quotient 0.3)* collapses (F3 −438 Hz): with a short weighted span the backward half of the fit is essential, as Gowda et al. argue.
  - *400–800 Hz*: every method fails, the reference included (F1 median |error| 63–111 Hz), as D-043's addendum.
  - *Not done*: changing a setting until a rate passes would fit the gate. A second attempt needs its own entry and fresh validation material not used here (other tract shapes, a child's tract among them, other vowels and source seeds), with the rate fixed in advance.


## D-047 (2026-10-29, pre-registered): does aspiration level move teacher v3's F1 bias on synthetic renders?

D-045's addendum: on PTDB-TUG dev, teacher v3's F1 overread rises with Praat HNR against all three references (women −0.1 → +14.7 Hz from the least to the most harmonic quartile, men +3.1 → +10.0), and the references' differences don't track HNR. Hannah: test it on renders, where the noise level can be changed alone. Earlier findings (D-034's sweeps, on the fidelity dashboard): on *steady* synthetic vowels with no aspiration, teacher v3 reads F1 as a staircase locked near harmonics, and 0 dB aspiration removes it; on real VocalSet singing there is no locking at all. Those sweeps compared no noise against an extreme one on unnatural vowels. This entry asks whether the noise level within the training range shifts the *signed bias* on natural renders.

- *Renders*: fresh clips from train_v2's synthesis config (teacher v3's synthetic training pool), seed 4547 (train_v2 is seed 2, so never trained on), no channel. Each clip is regenerated once per arm with `hnr_db` fixed. The generator still draws `hnr_db` from a one-point range, so its random stream is unchanged. *Checked*: on two clips, the voiced component and every label are identical between 5 and 40 dB, and the aspiration's RMS ratio is 56.2, as set.
- *Clips*: chosen by labels alone (arm-independent): not whole-clip whispered, median label-voiced f0 165–300 Hz (**women's range**, the first 150) or 90–160 Hz (**men's range**, the first 100), in index order.
- *Arms*: `hnr_db` 5, 10, 20, 30, 40 dB (training draws 5–40) and 60 dB (cleaner than any training clip).
- *Measure*: teacher v3 (`conformer_v2r2_s1_d16k`, whole-file `analyse`) against the label F1 on label-voiced, non-whispered frames with finite F1, 5 frames trimmed at each end; per clip the median signed error (Hz). Also the median |error| and Praat HNR (cc, as D-045) per clip and arm, to place the arms on the real voices' scale (PTDB-TUG women's Praat HNR quartiles 12.5 / 16.4 / 19.9 dB; synthetic training frames at f0 165–400 Hz 8.1 / 13.8 / 18.6).
- **Rule** (women's range): the paired difference in per-clip median signed error, 40 dB − 5 dB, mean over clips with a 95 % clip bootstrap (2,000).
  - Lower bound ≥ +4 Hz → **"cleaner excitation raises F1 on renders"**: the noise level reaches the bias directly, which supports a source-noise part in the real-voice link. Next: compare the training HNR distribution with real voices' (its own entry).
  - Interval inside ±4 Hz → **"no HNR effect on renders"**: the real-voice link goes through something that correlates with HNR (vowel nucleus, level, closure), not the noise level itself.
  - Upper bound ≤ −4 Hz → "opposite"; otherwise "inconclusive".
  - ±4 Hz is half of the women's +8 Hz closed-phase bias, as in D-046.
- *Reported*: every arm's mean and median signed error, |error| and Praat HNR; paired 20 − 5, 40 − 20 and 60 − 40 dB (the last outside the training range); the men's range likewise (reported only).
- *Noise floor, checked before registering* (`pilot`: the 20 dB arm only; seen: the per-clip spread and one clip's error, no contrast): per-clip median error SD 5.0 Hz (women, 150 clips), 3.2 Hz (men, 100), so a paired interval's half-width is at most 0.8 / 0.6 Hz, well inside ±4.
- *Limits*: one synthesiser, whose noise has a fixed spectral shape relative to the harmonics (pulsed, differentiated white noise through the tract). Real aperiodicity rises with frequency in a way that differs by phone class (SoFilNet's band aperiodicities, `docs/papers/literature.md`), so a null here doesn't rule out a noise-*shape* effect. No channel, so the channel augmentation's own noise is absent.
- *Code*: `scripts/analysis/d047_hnr_renders.py` (`check`, `pilot`, `run`). The 20 dB rows of the pilot are reused, since rendering is deterministic.
- **Result (2026-10-29 11:47; `reports/analysis/d047/d047.txt`): "no HNR effect on renders".** Women's range, paired 40 − 5 dB: +0.7 [+0.1, +1.3] Hz, inside ±4.
  - *The whole curve is flat*: women's mean signed error −0.1, +0.1, +0.4, +0.6, +0.6, +0.6 Hz from 5 to 60 dB; men −0.3 to −0.8 Hz. Mean |error| rises slightly with cleaner excitation (women 7.3 → 8.5 Hz).
  - *The arms reach the real voices' range*: Praat HNR rises from 7.7 to 21.1 dB (women's range) and saturates there, where jitter, shimmer and vibrato set the limit, so 60 dB adds nothing (60 − 40: +0.0 Hz). PTDB-TUG women's top HNR quartile starts at 19.9 dB, so the renders do cover it.
  - *Reading*: on renders, teacher v3 is unbiased at every aspiration level, including real voices' most harmonic range, where on PTDB-TUG it overreads by +11 to +15 Hz. The noise level does not reach the bias. The real-voice HNR link goes through something that moves with HNR and that the renders lack or don't vary with it (vowel nucleus against transitions, level, closure, the noise's spectral shape: this entry's limits).


## D-048 (2026-10-29, pre-registered): does an aspiration shape matching real voices move teacher v3's F1 bias on renders?

D-045's follow-ups: renders are too periodic below 2 kHz (women's 1–2 kHz band periodic fraction 0.68 against real 0.53) and agree above 2 kHz; whatever smears their harmonic detail makes them look more real, while more of our aspiration makes them look less real. D-047 varied only the aspiration *level* at our fixed shape (the first difference of white noise, about +6 dB/octave before the tract) and found no effect on F1; its 5 dB arm reached the real 1–2 kHz value only by making the bands above 2 kHz far too aperiodic. Hannah: test the shape.

- *Synthesiser change* (no effect on any existing set): `SynthConfig.asp_tilt_db_oct` (fixed, no random draw; default None = the difference, as before) and `synthesize(..., asp_tilt_db_oct)`, which shapes the *same* white-noise draw by a tilt in dB per octave (re 1 kHz, flat below 50 Hz). Level is still set by `hnr_db` over voiced samples. The interactive source refuses it. *Checked*: tests (`test_aspiration_tilt_changes_only_the_aspiration`): voiced component and labels identical, aspiration energy equal, more of it below 1 kHz at −6 dB/octave; all 36 synthesis tests pass.
- *Calibration, before registering* (`reports/analysis/d048/calib.txt`; band profile only, no F1 measured): 30 separate women's-range clips (seed 4550, never in the test), clean, tilt {difference, 0, −6, −12 dB/octave} × HNR {5, 10, 15, 20, 30 dB}; distance = RMS over the five bands to real women's mean profile (follow-up A, Zeroth and speechocean762 averaged: 0.908 / 0.527 / 0.286 / 0.127 / 0.042). Best: **−6 dB/octave at 10 dB** (0.899 / 0.531 / 0.264 / 0.090 / 0.005; distance 0.025); best with today's shape: the difference at 15 dB (0.970 / 0.610 / 0.168 / 0.026 / −0.007; 0.086); no other combination below 0.067.
- *Arms* (fixed by the calibration): **reference** = the difference at 20 dB (D-047's arm, re-rendered here for its band profile; its errors must reproduce D-047's 20 dB rows, checked in the report); **level_matched** = the difference at 15 dB; **shape_matched** = −6 dB/octave at 10 dB.
- *Renders, clips, measure*: exactly D-047's (fresh train_v2 renders, seed 4547, no channel, the same 150 women's-range and 100 men's-range clips; teacher v3 whole-file `analyse` against label F1, label-voiced non-whispered frames, 5 trimmed each end; per clip the median signed error). Band periodic fraction per clip and arm reported, to confirm the match on the test clips.
- **Rule** (women's range): paired shape_matched − reference, mean over clips, 95 % clip bootstrap (2,000). Lower bound ≥ +4 Hz → **"matched noise shape raises F1 on renders"**: real voices' noise shape reaches the bias, a candidate cause of part of the overread and a synthesiser change to test in training (its own entry). Interval inside ±4 → **"no noise-shape effect on renders"**. Upper bound ≤ −4 → "lowers"; otherwise "inconclusive". ±4 Hz as D-046 and D-047.
- *Reported*: level_matched − reference and shape_matched − level_matched (shape at a matched fit against level alone); |error|; men's range likewise.
- *Noise floor*: D-047's pilot on these clips (per-clip SD 5.0 / 3.2 Hz; paired half-width at most 0.8 / 0.6 Hz).
- *Limits*: one tilt for the whole noise; the timing (a closure peak) and wander within a clip unchanged; women's profile used for both sexes (men's real profile is less periodic still at 0–2 kHz); clean renders matched to real recordings, whose recording chain also lowers periodicity, so the shape_matched arm may slightly overshoot the source's true aperiodicity.
- *Code*: `scripts/analysis/d048_noise_tilt.py` (`calib`, `run`).
- **Result (2026-10-29 14:27; `reports/analysis/d048/d048.txt`): "no noise-shape effect on renders".** Women's range, paired shape_matched − reference: −2.0 [−2.7, −1.4] Hz, inside ±4. The re-rendered reference reproduces D-047's 20 dB rows (max |difference| 0.001 Hz).
  - *The match held on the test clips*: shape_matched's band periodic fraction 0.890 / 0.551 / 0.258 / 0.088 / 0.025 against real women's 0.907 / 0.527 / 0.286 / 0.127 / 0.042 (reference 0.971 / 0.719 / 0.266 / 0.045 / 0.014).
  - *Level alone does nothing* (level_matched − reference −0.1 [−0.2, +0.1]); the shape lowers F1 slightly (shape_matched − level_matched −2.0 [−2.5, −1.4]), the opposite of the real-voice overread. Men: +0.6 [−0.1, +1.3]. Mean |error| unchanged (women 7.7 → 7.3 Hz).
  - *Reading*: with real voices' aperiodicity profile, teacher v3 stays unbiased on renders. Neither the level (D-047) nor the shape (here) of the source noise produces the overread, so it lies in something else that renders lack: the dynamics resynthesis brings (D-045 follow-up B3), the source pulse and closure, or the definition of F1 itself (the effective, coupled resonance in real voices, against the closed-glottis one in our labels, D-023). The tilt stays in the synthesiser as an option for realism (follow-up A), with no claim on the bias.


## D-049 (2026-10-29, pre-registered): teacher v3's F1 bias on resynthesised real speech, by sex

`docs/f1_bias_register.md`, "Dynamics": synthetic contours are unnatural (Hannah), and D-045's probes found resynthesised clips much more real-looking than renders. Teacher v3 is unbiased on identity resynthesis of real *singing* (VocalSet val, sexes pooled: +0.0 Hz); real *speech* by sex has not been measured. Resynthesis keeps a recording's real source (inverse-filtered) and real f0, level and articulation dynamics, and replaces the tract with a perturbed synthetic one whose labels are exact (D-016, D-019).

- *Clips*: `data/resynth/zeroth_korean_val_d16k` (10 held-out val speakers, recorded sex 6 women / 4 men; numeric ids only), audio as stored, no channel. The store keeps no clip-to-file map, so only clips from shards whose 8 files share one speaker are used: 1,880 of 2,084 clips (52 of 58 shards). The flat store follows the sorted shards (`consolidate`), checked against the index's clip count.
- *Measure*: whole-clip `analyse`; per clip the median signed F1 error (model − label) on label-voiced frames with finite F1, 5 frames trimmed each end, clips with at least 20 such frames. Per sex, the mean over speakers of each speaker's mean clip error (speakers weighted equally), with a two-level bootstrap (speakers, then clips within them; 2,000).
- *Models*: **teacher v3** (`conformer_v2r2_s1_d16k`, trained with 30 % Zeroth-train and VocalSet resynthesis) and, reported beside it, **no_resynth** (`conformer_v2_s1_d16k`: same seed and recipe, train_v2 only). The second separates "real speech dynamics cause the bias" from "teacher v3 learned this domain from its resynthesised training data".
- **Rule** (teacher v3, women): lower bound ≥ +4 Hz → **"bias appears on resynthesised speech"** (real dynamics and source with exact labels reproduce the overread: dynamics or the real source are implicated); interval inside ±4 → **"no bias on resynthesised speech"** (with real source and dynamics, teacher v3 reads the labelled tract correctly, which leaves the real tract and F1's definition); upper bound ≤ −4 → "underread"; otherwise "inconclusive".
- *Noise floor, before registering* (pilot on the **men** only; seen: teacher v3's men's result): +0.7 [−0.6, +3.1] Hz, speakers −0.4 to +3.1, within-speaker clip SD 6.1 Hz. With 6 women's speakers the interval should be about ±2 Hz, inside the ±4 band.
- *Limits*: teacher v3 trained on Zeroth *train* resynthesis, the same method and corpus, so it may have learned this domain; no_resynth is the control for that. The resynthesis tract is perturbed (all poles × 0.8–1.25, per-formant offsets), so it is not the speaker's own; only source and dynamics are real. Korean read speech.
- *Code*: `scripts/analysis/d049_resynth_speech.py` (`pilot`, `run`; teacher v3's pilot clips are cached and reused).
- **Result (2026-10-29 15:13; `reports/analysis/d049/d049.txt`): "no bias on resynthesised speech".** Teacher v3, women: −0.0 [−0.8, +0.7] Hz (speakers −1.3 to +1.2); men +0.7 [−0.6, +3.3].
  - *Control*: no_resynth **underreads**: women −5.4 [−6.9, −3.8], men −4.1 [−5.7, −2.9], with a wider spread (mean |error| 21.5 against 12.8 Hz). The same model overreads real women's voices against the closed-phase reference (D-025: about +13 to +15 Hz at d16k). So real source and real speech dynamics do not produce an overread in either model; resynthesis training removed an *underread* on this domain, not the real-voice overread.
  - *Reading*: with the real source and dynamics and an exactly labelled synthetic tract, the bias is absent. The overread therefore lies in what resynthesis replaces: the real vocal tract (real bandwidths, wall vibration, coupling to the subglottal system and the open-phase shift) or F1's definition on real voices (`docs/f1_bias_register.md`; `considering.md`, Heads: a whole-cycle effective F1 head).
  - *Caveat*: the resynthesis source is the recording inverse-filtered with a tracker's formants, so it carries what the inverse filter left of the real tract (clips with |tract_left| > 0.5 were rejected at build); the real tract is mostly, not wholly, removed.

## D-050 (2026-10-30, design agreed; thresholds registered per probe): roadmap item 7, the invariance and equivariance probe suite: design

*Status*: **design agreed with Hannah (2026-10-30)**; nothing had run when she agreed it (only a renderer smoke test checking that the label formants and f0 move by exactly the imposed factors). It fixes what the suite measures and how it is built. Each probe's decision thresholds are registered separately, after its noise floor is measured (item 7's rule), and before the lockbox opens. 5c waits for this suite (roadmap, Hannah 2026-10-30).

- *Why one suite*: the probes so far are one-off scripts (`invariance_run.py`, `channel_probe.py`, `d037_cache.py`'s probe and shift parts, `d038_score.py`, `d041_invariance.py`), each with its own clips and measures. Item 7 needs every model scored on identical, cached inputs with the same measures and one report, so v3, v3.1 and 5c can be compared in one go.
- *Structure*: `vocal_analysis/eval/probes/`. A probe is a family of **paired conditions**: each source item is rendered under every condition, and each condition carries the imposed change, as a log factor for each output it should move (0 for invariance). Rendered audio is cached per probe with a fixed seed (`data/probes/<probe>/`), so every model hears the same samples. A runner scores a checkpoint (`va probes <ckpt>`) into `reports/probes/<run>.md/.json`, as the suite does.
- *Measures, per output* (F1–F4, B1–B4, VTL; f0 and voicing where they apply): per source item, the slope of log output against the imposed log change across its conditions (equivariance: ideal 1; invariance: the slope or the change against the reference condition, ideal 0); the median over items with a bootstrap over speakers or source recordings; a **shuffle null** (conditions permuted within item) for every slope; and **σ growth**: the change in the model's own σ against the reference condition, so an output that loses evidence should widen rather than drift. On real audio with a reference (channel probes on gold sets) the change in error against the reference is reported too.
- *Noise floor*: the shuffle null per measure, and seed spread from teacher v3's three seeds (`conformer_v2r2_{,s1_,s2_}d16k`) and v3.1's two (`d040_5b_s{1,2}_b20k`). Thresholds come from these; none is set from the models' probe scores.
- *Probes, in build order*:
  1. **Counterfactual resynthesis** (non-gold held-out pools: Zeroth-Korean val, VocalSet val; teacher v2's cached analyses): the real inverse-filtered source re-filtered through a deterministic tract (smoothed tracks, no random offsets or bandwidth draws). **Length**: α ∈ {0.85, 0.92, 1, 1.09, 1.18} (formants × 1/α; VTL × α); **f0**: source resampled by r ∈ {0.84, 0.92, 1, 1.09, 1.19}, tracks time-warped to match (this also changes the speaking rate by 1/r, which the timing probe separates); **both**, crossed at the outer values. *Checks before use*: α = r = 1 against the original recording (resynthesis artefacts); and the **source residue**: what the inverse filter left of the real tract (`tract_left` β) does not scale with α, so length equivariance is biased towards 0 by the residue's share. The probe reports its slopes against β and on clips with |β| < 0.2, which also gives the held tract_left dose test its natural home.
  2. **Single-formant shift on resynthesis**: D-038's probe with the real source instead of the synthetic pulse.
  3. **Channel** (dev gold audio only, Hillenbrand and PTDB-TUG dev; paired against the clean file): codecs from `synth/codecs.py`'s table at fixed bitrates; telephone band; microphone responses (`synth/channel.py`'s peaking-filter EQ at fixed settings); reverberation, T60 ∈ {0.2, 0.4, 0.8} s; additive noise at SNR ∈ {30, 20, 10, 5} dB (white, pink, babble). Invariance with σ growth; error against the reference.
  4. **Timing**: sub-hop offsets (k/8 hop), polarity inversion, crop position and context length, time-stretch without pitch change.
  5. **Time reversal with a change of speaker** (Hannah): A-then-B joins, forward against reversed per frame, by distance from the join; dry audio, whole-hop padding, symmetric windows (`considering.md`).
  6. **Voice quality and effort** (synthetic renders with exact truth): Rd, aspiration (D-047's level and D-048's shape), jitter and shimmer, spectral tilt.
  The existing synthetic f0 probe (D-020) and shift probe (D-038) move into the suite unchanged, so their history carries over.
- *Data rules*: gold audio is used only for evaluation and only dev speakers; nothing here is trained on or tunes a parameter. Zeroth: numeric ids only.
- *Cost*: rendering is CPU; scoring one model on every probe should take minutes on the GPU.
- *Amendment (Hannah, 2026-10-30 ~01:30; seen beforehand: probe 1's exploratory scores for v3 and v3.1, committed in `dc8b41b`)*: four more probes, built in this order before the rest of the list:
  7. **Bandwidth counterfactual** (probe 1's renderer, bandwidths × s ∈ {0.7, 0.85, 1.2, 1.45, 2.0}, formants fixed): F1–F4, VTL and f0 should not move (ideal slope 0 against log2 s); bandwidths should follow the labels (ideal 1). Prompted by probe 1's side finding that the models read real voices' B1 and B2 1.4–1.8 × wider than the synthesis prior: if F1 rises with bandwidth, that is a mechanism for the women's overread.
  8. **Extra pole-zero pairs**: each item re-rendered with a nasal pair and, separately, subglottal pairs, drawn from the training distribution (`synth.tract.sample_extras` with p = 1, seeded per item). F1–F4 should not move (change against the reference, ideal 0), σ may grow.
  9. **Signal chain** (on the original clips): gain (× 0.1, × 0.5, × 2, no clipping), DC offset, hard clipping at half the peak, and a 16 → 44.1 → 16 kHz round trip. Every output should be unchanged (ideal 0).
  10. **Instantaneous against anatomical length**: larynx height and lip protrusion move the formants while the anatomical length stays (needs 5c's `pg_cm` / `pm_cm` labels; built with 5c's preparation, so 5c can be scored on it).

- *First measurements, exploratory (2026-10-30 night and morning; nothing is decided from them; thresholds come from the noise floors, registered per probe)*. Seven models: v3.1 (`d040_5b_s2_b20k`) and its sibling seed (`_s1_`), v3 (`conformer_v2r2_s1_d16k`) and its sibling seeds (`conformer_v2r2_d16k`, `_s2_`), v1, v2; 60 items (Zeroth-Korean val, 10 speakers; probe 10: 60 synthetic tube clips). Reports in `reports/probes/<probe>/`.
  - **Probe 1, counterfactual**: length equivariance 0.97–0.99 for F1–F4 in every Conformer, and VTL 0.99 / 0.99 (v3.1's two seeds; nulls ±0.16). f0 arm, F1 against log2 f0: v3 seeds +0.021, +0.024, +0.023; v3.1 seeds +0.007, +0.021; v1 −0.010, v2 +0.033; every interval includes 0. So v3.1's +0.007 is within seed spread. σ widens where evidence thins (lower F1, higher f0, F4 near the band edge). The original recording against its α = r = 1 resynthesis: B1 / B2 read 1.4–1.8 × the synthesis prior's bandwidths (men more than women), while the resynthesis reads at 0.95–0.97 ×: real bandwidths are wider than the prior, or read so (a lead for `f1_bias_register.md`, not sex-specific in the overread's direction).
  - **Probe 2, single-formant shift on resynthesised speech** (D-038's factors 0.9, 0.95, 1.05, 1.1; frames where the moved formant comes within 1.10 of a neighbour left out): the moved formant follows its label change with slopes 0.92–0.98 in every model, and the unmoved ones drag by at most 0.03. VTL leans on the upper formants: an F4-only shift moves it with slopes −0.27 and −0.34 (v3.1's two seeds). Reports `reports/probes/shift/`.
  - **Probe 3, channel** (Hillenbrand dev, 996 files; PTDB-TUG dev, 240 files; 25 conditions paired against the clean file; medians with a speaker bootstrap; reports `reports/probes/channel/`). Codecs, the 4 kHz low-pass and 30–20 dB noise move F1–F4 by a few cents at most. Exceptions:
    - **A ±6 dB peak at 1 kHz moves F1 by +11 to +13 / −12 to −14 cents in every model**, and cutting 1 kHz lowers Hillenbrand F1 error by 2–6 Hz while boosting it raises the error by 4–6. Energy between F1 and F2 pulls F1 up; this is a lead for the women's F1 overread (register row), not a test of it.
    - Noise at 10–5 dB lowers F1 by up to 12 cents (16 for v2) and also lowers its error (−3 to −7 Hz), the same direction as the 1 kHz cut. F3 and F4 errors grow with noise (F3 +90 to +117 Hz at 5 dB, every model), but the Conformers' F3 median stays within 7 cents where v1 drifts −30 and v2 −19. The models widen σ rather than drift: F1 σ log2 +0.2 to +0.5 at 5 dB, F2–F4 up to +1.5.
    - Reverberation costs f0 RPA 2–7 points in every model alike (the largest f0 effect here); otherwise RPA moves ≤ 1.7 points. Under reverberation v3.1's two seeds read F1 8–10 cents low, while v3's three seeds scatter (−7 to +6); the F2 error grows 5–10 Hz in every Conformer.
    - AMR-NB at 7.4 kbit/s and the telephone band raise F1 by 3–10 cents in the Conformers (not v1 or v2) and lower F3 by 7–11 (v1 −33).
    - Clean errors match the suite (v3.1 F1–F4 39/42/43/97 Hz, PTDB RPA 87.1).
  - **Probe 7, bandwidth**: F1–F4 do not move with bandwidth in any model (slopes within ±0.003, intervals about ±0.007): **the bandwidth mechanism for the women's overread is ruled out on resynthesised speech**. Bandwidth outputs follow only half the imposed change (B1–B4 slopes 0.42–0.63 for the Conformers, 0.32–0.42 for v1): bandwidth estimates are compressed towards the prior.
  - **Probe 8, pole-zero pairs**: nasal pairs move F1 by +0 to +6 cents, subglottal pairs F2 by −3 to −8 cents (v1 the most); small against D-033's 5 Hz bars.
  - **Probe 9, signal chain**: DC offset, clipping at half the peak and a 44.1 kHz round trip move nothing beyond ±2 cents. Gain (× 0.1 to × 2): Conformers within ±3 cents; **v1 F1 −21 cents and v2 −5**: the older GRU teachers were not gain invariant.
  - **Probe 10, articulation**: lip changes move F1–F4 with the labels (slopes 0.98–1.07); larynx changes move F2–F4 with the labels, while F1's slope is unstable (intervals about ±0.6: a larynx change barely moves F1, so its x is tiny). **The anatomical VTL head follows the instantaneous length: larynx slope +1.01 and +1.11, lips +0.89 and +0.87 (v3.1's two seeds; ideal 0).** Performed shortening currently reads as a different anatomy; this is 5c's baseline.

## D-051 (2026-10-30, registered with Hannah's choices before anything was built): roadmap 5c, long clips with sustained larynx and lip movement, and an instantaneous-length head

*Status*: **registered (Hannah, 2026-10-30 ~02:00)**: her answers fixed the head, the clip and crop lengths, the mix share, the movement ranges and the rule's form; the movement trajectories below are mine, within her ranges. Nothing had been generated or trained. 5c starts after D-050's probes 7–10 (Hannah: "work through the new probes, then start 5c").

- *Question*: does training on long clips where larynx height and lip protrusion move slowly, with the anatomical length fixed, plus a head that reads the instantaneous length, cost anything on what we already measure? Gains are reported, not required (Hannah: the 4 s crops may be what limits it, so no improvement is demanded).
- *Arms*: **5c** = teacher v3.1's recipe (5b: rA + joint VTL head) with the new data and head, seeds 1 and 2, against **5b** seeds 1 and 2 (`d040_5b_s{1,2}_b20k`), seeds paired. One addition: the long articulation clips and the instantaneous head together (neither does anything without the other).
- *Data*: **30 %** of training items (as resynthesis's share in v3, D-025) drawn from a new long-clip set; the other 70 % as 5b's mix in the same proportions. Long clips are **8–16 s**; **crops stay 4 s** (400 frames), as now and as the windowed analysis (Hannah: one change at a time). Each long clip keeps one speaker and one anatomical length; larynx height `pg_cm` and lip protrusion `pm_cm` follow **sustained trajectories within the current tube ranges** (`tube.sample_target`: pg uniform ± 0.5 cm × size, pm −0.3 to +1.0 cm × size): piecewise holds of 1–4 s joined by 0.2–1 s cosine transitions, each new level drawn from those ranges, with the vowel targets changing underneath as now. Wider ranges are a later arm (5d, Hannah) after this one, with sources for the ranges checked first; longer crops likewise.
- *Labels*: instantaneous length per frame = anatomical length + pg + pm (tier III adds length at the glottis and lips); the existing VTL head keeps the anatomical length (fixed per clip) as its target. **New head**: per-frame log2 instantaneous length with σ (Gaussian NLL), built like the VTL head (D-036: gate, weight 0.0125, own warm-up), on sonorant frames. On the other 70 %, instantaneous = anatomical + that target's pg + pm.
- *Measures*: everything D-040 measured, against 5b (synthetic F1–F4 and RPA; dev guard-rails; women's closed phase; Hillenbrand length reliability, w/m, probe excess); D-050's probes (counterfactual, bandwidth, pole-zero, signal chain, and probe 10, which this set enables); the new head's validation error and coverage on held-out long clips.
- *Rule* (**non-inferiority**, seed means, seeds paired; D-040's margins): 5c is adopted if (1) no formant cost (synthetic F1–F4 no worse than +2 SE, RPA not below −0.1; dev Hillenbrand F1 ≤ +4, F2 ≤ +9, F3 ≤ +20 Hz, PTDB-TUG RPA ≥ −1.0, women's closed-phase F1 ≤ +5 Hz); (2) length not worse: Hillenbrand reliability − 5b's paired interval lower bound above −0.02 (D-040's check margin), w/m in [0.85, 0.92]; (3) f0 probe excess ≤ 10 c/oct. Otherwise 5b stays. Seeds disagreeing → "not robust to the seed", as D-040. Probe results and the instantaneous head are reported; they decide nothing here.
- *Cost*: CPU generation of the long set; two runs of about 8 h each on the GPU.
- *Implementation (2026-10-30 ~03:00; no 5c run had started, nothing scored)*: `SynthConfig.articulation="sustained"` (`sampler._sustained`; pg and pm on independent timelines, levels uniform over `tube.PG_RANGE` / `PM_RANGE` × size, imposed on each tube target through `sample_target(pg_cm=, pm_cm=)`; no draws when off, and train_v2 clips were checked to reproduce bit for bit). Labels `length_inst_cm` from `generate`, and the same values without rendering from `sampler.clip_length_inst` (tested equal), written for any set by `scripts/analysis/inst_labels.py`. train_v2's tube clips already move: their per-target pg / pm draws give instantaneous lengths varying by up to about 1.5 cm within a clip, so the new head learns from the 70 % too. **The long set is all tube** (`p_tube` 1: the movement is the tube's tier III, so a cascade clip would carry none); otherwise train_v2's settings (`scripts/analysis/make_long_set.py`: 25,000 clips of 8–16 s, about train_v2's audio hours, seed 5051, `data/synth/train_long_v1`). The instantaneous head is a second `vtl_head_module` (`TeacherConfig.vtl_inst_head`) on the same gated input, in the VTL parameter group, initialised with `vtl_seed + 1`; `TrainConfig.vtl_inst`, `synth_extra`, `synth_extra_frac` (the rest scaled by 1 − 0.3, so train_v2 49 %, resynthesis 21 %). The long set's last 2 % is held out as `eval_extra:train_long_v1`. `va train --train-set` now parses bools and tuples (no earlier chain passed either). Chain: `chains/d051.sh`.
- *Chain restart (2026-10-30 ~06:00; nothing trained)*: the chain's first step reran `make_long_set.py` on the finished set and `write_set` refused it: the set's `index.json` records `artic_pg_cm` / `artic_pm_cm` as `None`, the default when generation started, and the default became 0.0 before the commit. Only `articulation="fixed"` reads those fields. Clips from shards 0, 40 and 97, regenerated with the committed code, matched the stored int16 audio and `length_inst_cm` labels exactly, so the set stands as written. The step now skips when the last shard exists.
